, we show the proof of the claim in
Example
2
.

A.1.2
Analysis regarding the Eluder Dimension

For simplicity, we denote
x_{h}^{k}=(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})
.

First we have two technical lemma. The first lemma bounds the summation of “self-normalization" terms by the Eluder dimension. Our result generalizes the previous result by
[
285
]
from the
\ell_{2}
-Eluder dimension to the
\ell_{1}
case.

Lemma A.1.1
.

Suppose for all
g\in\Psi,|g|\leq 1
and
\lambda>1
, then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\min\bigg\{1,\sup_{g\in\Psi}\frac{|g(x_{h}^{k})|}{\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k^{\prime}})|+\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k})|+\lambda}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq 12\log^{2}(4\lambda KH)\cdot DE_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/(8\lambda KH))+\lambda^{-1}.

Proof
[Proof of
Lemma
A.1.1
]
We follow the proof steps of Theorem 4.6 in
[
285
]
.
For simplicity, we use
n=KH
,
i=kH+h
to denote the indices and denote
x_{h}^{k}
by
x_{i}
. Then we need to prove

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\min\bigg\{1,\sup_{g\in\Psi}\frac{|g(x_{i})|}{\sum_{t=1}^{i-1}|g(x_{t})|+\lambda}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq 12\log^{2}(4\lambda n)\cdot DE_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/(8\lambda n))+\lambda^{-1}

(A.1)

Let

\displaystyle g_{i}=\argmax_{g\in\Psi}\frac{|g(x_{i})|}{\sum_{j=1}^{i-1}|g(x_{j})|+\lambda}

(A.2)

For any
1/(\lambda n)\leq\rho\leq 1
and
1\leq j\leq\lceil\log(4\lambda n)\rceil
, we define

\displaystyle A_{\rho}^{j}=\bigg\{i\in[n]:2^{-j}<|g_{i}(x_{i})|\leq 2^{-j+1},\frac{|g_{i}(x_{i})|}{\sum_{t=1}^{i-1}|g_{i}(x_{t})|+\lambda}\geq\rho/2\bigg\},

\displaystyle d_{j}:=DE_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},2^{-j}).

(A.3)

Next we only consider the set
A_{\rho}^{j}
where
|A_{\rho}^{j}|>d_{j}
. We denote
A_{\rho}^{j}=\{a_{1},\dots,a_{A}\}
, where
A=|A_{\rho}^{j}|
and
\{a_{i}\}
keeps the same order as
\{x_{i}\}
. Next we do the following constructions. We maintain
k=\lfloor(A-1)/d_{j}\rfloor
number of queues
Q_{1},\dots,Q_{k}
, all of them initialized as emptysets. We put
a_{1}
into
Q_{1}
. For
a_{i},i\geq 2
, we put
a_{i}
into
Q_{l}
, where
Q_{l}
is the first queue where
a_{i}
is
2^{-j}
-independent of all elements in
Q_{l}
. Let
i_{\max}
be the smallest
i
when we can not put
a_{i}
into any existing queue.

We claim that
i_{\max}
indeed exists, i.e., our construction will stop before we put all elements in
A^{j}_{\rho}
into
Q_{1},\dots,Q_{k}
. In fact, note the fact that the length of each
Q_{l}
is always no more than
d_{j}
, which is due to the fact that any
2^{-j}
-independent sequence’s length is at most
d_{j}
. Meanwhile, since we only have
k=\lfloor(A-1)/d_{j}\rfloor
, then the amount of elements in
Q_{1}\cup\dots\cup Q_{k}
will be upper bounded by
k\cdot d_{j}<A
. That suggests at least one element in
A_{\rho}^{j}
is not contained by
Q_{1}\cup\dots\cup Q_{k}
, i.e.,
i_{\max}
exists.

By the definition of
i_{\max}
, we know that
a_{i_{\max}}
is
2^{-j}
-dependent to each
Q_{l}
. Next we give a bound of
A
. First, note

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{i_{\max}-1}|g_{i_{\max}}(x_{t})|\geq\sum_{t\in Q_{1}\cup\dots\cup Q_{k}}|g_{i_{\max}}(a_{t})|=\sum_{l=1}^{k}\sum_{t\in Q_{l}}|g_{i_{\max}}(a_{t})|>k\cdot 2^{-j},

(A.4)

where the first inequality holds since
Q_{l}
are the elements that appear before
a_{i_{\max}}
, the second one holds due to the following induction of Eluder dimension: since
a_{i_{\max}}
is
2^{-j}
-dependent to
Q_{l}
, then we have

\displaystyle\forall g\in\Psi,\ \sum_{t\in Q_{l}}|g(a_{t})|\leq 2^{-j}\Rightarrow|g(a_{i_{\max}})|\leq 2^{-j}.

(A.5)

Therefore, given the fact
|g_{i_{\max}}(a_{i_{\max}})|>2^{-j}
(recall the definition of
A^{j}_{\rho}
), we must have
\sum_{t\in Q_{l}}|g_{i_{\max}}(a_{t})|>2^{-j}
as well, which suggests the second inequality of
Equation
A.4
holds. Second, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{i_{\max}-1}|g_{i_{\max}}(x_{t})|\leq 2/\rho\cdot|g_{i_{\max}}(a_{i_{\max}})|\leq 4\cdot 2^{-j}/\rho,

(A.6)

where both inequalities hold due to the definition of
A^{j}_{\rho}
. Combining
Equation
A.4
and
Equation
A.6
, we have

\displaystyle k<4/\rho\Rightarrow A\leq 4d_{j}/\rho+d_{j}\leq 5d_{j}/\rho.

(A.7)

Therefore, we have that for all
\rho,j
,
|A^{j}_{\rho}|\leq 5d_{j}/\rho
.

Finally we prove
Equation
A.1
.
1/(\lambda n)\leq\rho\leq 1
and
1\leq j\leq\lceil\log(4\lambda n)\rceil=J
. Denote

\displaystyle A_{\rho}=\bigg\{i\in[n]:\frac{|g_{i}(x_{i})|}{\sum_{t=1}^{i-1}|g_{i}(x_{t})|+\lambda}\geq\rho/2\bigg\}.

(A.8)

Then it is easy to notice that
|A_{\rho}|=\sum_{j}|A^{j}_{\rho}|\leq\lceil\log(4\lambda n)\rceil\cdot 5d_{J}/\rho
, where we use the fact that the Eluder dimension
d_{j}
is increasing. Therefore, by the standard peeling technique, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\min\bigg\{1,\sup_{g\in\Psi}\frac{|g(x_{i})|}{\sum_{t=1}^{i-1}|g(x_{t})|+\lambda}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in[\lceil\log(\lambda n)\rceil]}\sum_{i\in A_{2^{-j}}\setminus A_{2^{-j+1}}}+\sum_{j=\lceil\log(\lambda n)\rceil}\sum_{i\notin A_{2^{-j+1}}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in[\lceil\log(\lambda n)\rceil]}\sum_{i\in A_{2^{-j}}\setminus A_{2^{-j+1}}}2^{-j-1}+n\cdot 1/(\lambda n)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in[\lceil\log(\lambda n)\rceil]}\sum_{i\in A_{2^{-j}}}2^{-j-1}+n\cdot 1/(\lambda n)

\displaystyle\leq\lceil\log(\lambda n)\rceil\cdot\lceil\log(4\lambda n)\rceil\cdot 3d_{J}+\lambda^{-1},

which concludes our proof.

Next lemma gives a bound to bound the number of episodes where the behavior along these episodes are “bad". Intuitively speaking, our lemma suggests we only have limited number of bad episodes, therefore won’t affect the final performance of our algorithm.

Lemma A.1.2
.

Given
\lambda>1
. There exists at most

\displaystyle 13\log^{2}(4\lambda KH)\cdot DE_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/(8\lambda KH))

(A.9)

number of
k\in[K]
satisfying the following claim

\displaystyle\sup_{g\in\Psi}\frac{\lambda+\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k^{\prime}})|}{\lambda+\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k^{\prime}})|}>4.

(A.10)

Proof
[Proof of
Lemma
A.1.2
]

Note that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\min\bigg\{2,\log\sup_{g\in\Psi}\frac{\lambda+\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k^{\prime}})|}{\lambda+\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k^{\prime}})|}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\min\bigg\{2,\log\prod_{h=1}^{H}\sup_{g\in\Psi}\frac{\lambda+\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k^{\prime}})|+\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k})|}{\lambda+\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k^{\prime}})|+\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k})|}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\min\bigg\{2,\sum_{h=1}^{H}\log\bigg(1+\sup_{g\in\Psi}\frac{|g(x_{h}^{k})|}{\lambda+\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k^{\prime}})|+\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k})|}\bigg)\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\min\bigg\{2,\sup_{g\in\Psi}\frac{|g(x_{h}^{k})|}{\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k^{\prime}})|+\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k})|+\lambda}\bigg\},

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\min\bigg\{1,\sup_{g\in\Psi}\frac{|g(x_{h}^{k})|}{\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k^{\prime}})|+\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{h-1}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k})|+\lambda}\bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq 24\log^{2}(4\lambda KH)\cdot DE_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/(8\lambda KH))+2\lambda^{-1}

\displaystyle\leq 26\log^{2}(4\lambda KH)\cdot DE_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/(8\lambda KH)).

(A.11)

where the first inequality holds since
\sup_{g}\prod f(g)\leq\prod\sup_{g}f(g)
, the second one holds since
\log(1+x)\leq x
, the fourth one holds due to
Lemma
A.1.1
. Therefore, there are at most

\displaystyle 26\log^{2}(4\lambda KH)\cdot DE_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/(8\lambda KH))/2

number of
k
satisfying

\displaystyle\log\sup_{g\in\Psi}\frac{\lambda+\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k^{\prime}})|}{\lambda+\sum_{k^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h^{\prime}=1}^{H}|g(x_{h^{\prime}}^{k^{\prime}})|}>2,

which concludes the proof.

We next have the following lemma, which bounds the regret by the Eluder dimension.

Lemma A.1.3

(Theorem 5.3,
[
227
]
)
.

Let
C:=\sup_{(s,a)\in{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},f\in\Psi}\left|f((s,a))\right|
be the envelope.
For any sequences
f^{(1)},\dots,f^{(N)}\subseteq\Psi
,
(s,a)^{(1)},\dots,(s,a)^{(N)}\subseteq{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A}
, let
\beta
be a constant such that for all
n\in[N]
we have,

\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\left|f^{(n)}((s,a)^{i})\right|\leq\beta.

Then, for all
n\in[N]
, we have

\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left|f^{(t)}((s,a)^{t})\right|\leq\inf_{0<\epsilon\leq 1}\left\{\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},\epsilon)(2C+\beta\log(C/\epsilon))+n\epsilon\right\}.

Given
Lemma
A.1.2
and Lemma
Lemma
A.1.3
, we are able to prove the following key lemma.

Lemma A.1.4

(New Eluder Pigeon Lemma)
.

Let the event
\mathcal{E}
be

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}:\forall k\in[K],\ \sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{H}^{2}(\widehat{P}^{k}(s_{h}^{i},a_{h}^{i})||P^{*}(s_{h}^{i},a_{h}^{i}))\leq\eta.

(A.12)

Then under event
\mathcal{E}
, there exists a set
\mathcal{K}\in[K]
such that

•

We have
|\mathcal{K}|\leq 13\log^{2}(4\eta KH)\cdot DE_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/(8\eta KH))
.

•

We have

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\mathrel{\|}\widehat{P}^{k}\big(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\inf_{0<\epsilon\leq 1}\left\{\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},\epsilon)(2+7\eta\log(1/\epsilon))+KH\epsilon\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)(2+7\eta\log(KH))+1\,,

(A.13)

where the function class
\Psi=\{(s,a)\mapsto\mathbb{H}^{2}(P^{\star}(s,a)\mathrel{\|}P(s,a)):P\in\mathcal{P}\}
.

Proof
[Proof of
Lemma
A.1.4
]
We interchangeably use
n=kH+h
to denote the indices of
s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k}
. We set
f^{(n)}((s,a))
in
Lemma
A.1.3
as
H^{2}(P^{k}(s,a)||P^{*}(s,a))
.

First, we prove that the
\beta
in
Lemma
A.1.3
can be selected as
7\eta
under event
\mathcal{E}
. To show that, let
\mathcal{K}
denote all the
k
stated in
Lemma
A.1.2
. Then for all
k
such that
k+1\notin\mathcal{K}
,
h=2,...,H
, let
n=kH+h
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\left|f^{(n)}((s,a)^{i})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=0}^{kH+H}\left|f^{(n)}((s,a)^{i})\right|

\displaystyle=\bigg(\lambda+\sum_{i=0}^{kH}\left|f^{(n)}((s,a)^{i})\right|\bigg)\cdot\frac{\sum_{i=0}^{kH+H}\left|f^{(n)}((s,a)^{i})\right|+\lambda}{\sum_{i=0}^{kH}\left|f^{(n)}((s,a)^{i})\right|+\lambda}-\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\lambda+\sum_{i=0}^{kH}\left|f^{(n)}((s,a)^{i})\right|\bigg)\cdot 4-\lambda

\displaystyle\leq 7\eta,

(A.14)

where the second inequality holds due to
Lemma
A.1.2
, the last one holds due to the definition of
\mathcal{E}
. Therefore, we prove our lemma by the conclusion of
Lemma
A.1.3
with
\beta=7\eta
.

A.1.3
Other Supporting Lemmas

Lemma A.1.5

(Simulation Lemma (
[
4
]
))
.

We have

\displaystyle V_{0;P^{\star}}^{\pi}-{V}_{0;\hat{P}}^{\pi}\leq\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi}_{h}}\left[\left|\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s,a)}V^{\pi}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}(s^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(s,a)}V^{\pi}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}(s^{\prime})\right|\right].

Lemma A.1.6

(Change of Variance Lemma (Lemma C.5 in
[
107
]
))
.

\displaystyle\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;P^{\star}}^{\pi}\big)(s,a)\right]=\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi}.

Lemma A.1.7

(Generalization bounds of MLE for finite model class (Theorem E.4 in
[
227
]
))
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be the context/feature space and
\mathcal{Y}
be the label space, and we are given a dataset
D=\left\{(x_{i},y_{i})\right\}_{i\in[n]}
from a martingale process:
for
i=1,2,...,n
, sample
x_{i}\sim\mathcal{D}_{i}(x_{1:i-1},y_{1:i-1})
and
y_{i}\sim p(\cdot\mid x_{i})
.
Let
f^{\star}(x,y)=p(y\mid x)
and we are given a realizable,
i.e.
,
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}
, function class
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\to\Delta(\mathbb{R})
of distributions.
Suppose
\mathcal{F}
is finite.
Fix any
\delta\in(0,1)
, set
\beta=\log(|\mathcal{F}|/\delta)
and define

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{F}}=\left\{f\in\mathcal{F}:\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log f(x_{i},y_{i})\geq\max_{\widetilde{f}\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\widetilde{f}(x_{i},y_{i})-4\beta\right\}.

Then w.p. at least
1-\delta
, the following holds:

(1)

The true distribution is in the version space,
i.e.
,
f^{\star}\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}
.

(2)

Any function in the version space is close to the ground truth data-generating distribution,
i.e.
, for all
f\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathcal{D}_{i}}\left[\mathbb{H}^{2}(f(x,\cdot)\mathrel{\|}f^{\star}(x,\cdot))\right]\leq 22\beta.

Lemma A.1.8

(Generalization bounds of MLE for infinite model class (Theorem E.5 in
[
227
]
))
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be the context/feature space and
\mathcal{Y}
be the label space, and we are given a dataset
D=\left\{(x_{i},y_{i})\right\}_{i\in[n]}
from a martingale process:
for
i=1,2,...,n
, sample
x_{i}\sim\mathcal{D}_{i}(x_{1:i-1},y_{1:i-1})
and
y_{i}\sim p(\cdot\mid x_{i})
.
Let
f^{\star}(x,y)=p(y\mid x)
and we are given a realizable,
i.e.
,
f^{\star}\in\mathcal{F}
, function class
\mathcal{F}:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\to\Delta(\mathbb{R})
of distributions.
Suppose
\mathcal{F}
is finite.
Fix any
\delta\in(0,1)
, set
\beta=\log(\mathcal{N}_{[]}((n|\mathcal{Y}|)^{-1},\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})/\delta)
(where
\mathcal{N}_{[]}((n|\mathcal{Y}|)^{-1},\mathcal{F},\|\cdot\|_{\infty})
is the bracketing number defined in
Definition
3.2
) and define

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{F}}=\left\{f\in\mathcal{F}:\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log f(x_{i},y_{i})\geq\max_{\widetilde{f}\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\log\widetilde{f}(x_{i},y_{i})-7\beta\right\}.

Then w.p. at least
1-\delta
, the following holds:

(1)

The true distribution is in the version space,
i.e.
,
f^{\star}\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}
.

(2)

Any function in the version space is close to the ground truth data-generating distribution,
i.e.
, for all
f\in\widehat{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{x\sim\mathcal{D}_{i}}\left[\mathbb{H}^{2}(f(x,\cdot)\mathrel{\|}f^{\star}(x,\cdot))\right]\leq 28\beta.

Lemma A.1.9

(Recursion Lemma)
.

Let
G>0
be a positive constant,
a<G/2
is also a positive constant, and let
\{C_{m}\}_{m=0}^{N=\lceil\log_{2}(\frac{KH}{G})\rceil}
be a sequence of positive real numbers satisfying:

[1]

C_{m}\leq 2^{m}G+\sqrt{aC_{m+1}}+a
for all
m\geq 0
,

[2]

C_{m}\leq KH
for all
m\geq 0
, where
K>0
and
H>0
are positive constants.

Then, it holds that:

C_{0}\leq 4G.

Proof
[Proof of
Lemma
A.1.9
]
We will prove by induction that for all
m\geq 0
,

C_{m}\leq 2^{m+2}G.

Then, for
m=0
, this would immediately show
C_{0}\leq 4G
.

1. The base case
m=N
:

Since
N=\lceil\log_{2}(\frac{KH}{G})\rceil
, it is obvious that
2^{N+2}G\geq KH
.
Thus,
C_{N}\leq KH\leq 2^{N+2}G
, the inequality holds for
m=N
.

2. The induction step:

Assume that for some
m\geq 0
, for
C_{m+1}
, we have:

C_{m+1}\leq 2^{m+1+2}G=2^{m+3}G.

Then, we have

\displaystyle C_{m}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{m}G+\sqrt{aC_{m+1}}+a

\displaystyle\leq 2^{m}G+\sqrt{a2^{m+3}G}+a

\displaystyle\leq 2^{m}G+\sqrt{\frac{G}{2}\cdot 2^{m+3}G}+\frac{G}{2}

\displaystyle=G\cdot(2^{m}+2^{m/2+1}+2^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq G\cdot(2^{m}+2^{m+1}+2^{m})

\displaystyle=2^{m+2}G\,.

(A.15)

Therefore, by induction, we have
for all
m\geq 0
,

C_{m}\leq 2^{m+2}G.

And the proof follows by setting
m=0
.

A.1.4
Detailed Proofs for the online setting in
Section
3.3

A.1.5
Proof of
Theorem
3.3.2

The following is the full proof of
Theorem
3.3.2
.

For notational simplicity, throughout this whole section, we denote

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right],

\displaystyle C_{m}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}(V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}-V_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]

\displaystyle G

\displaystyle:=\sqrt{\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]\cdot\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)

\displaystyle I_{h}^{k}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{*}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})-V^{\pi^{k}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s_{h+1}^{k})

(A.16)

We use
\mathbb{I}\{\cdot\}
to denote the indicator function. We define the following events which we will later show that they happen with high probability.

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}

\displaystyle:=\{\forall k\in[K-1]:P^{\star}\in\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{k},\text{and}\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{H}^{2}(P^{\star}(s_{h}^{i},a_{h}^{i})||\widehat{P}^{k}(s_{h}^{i},a_{h}^{i}))\leq 22\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta).\}\,,

(A.17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\Big\{\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}I_{h}^{k}\lesssim\sqrt{\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\log(1/\delta)}+\log(1/\delta)\Big\}\,,

(A.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{3}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{E}_{1}\cap\{\forall m\in[0,\lceil\log_{2}(\frac{KH}{G})\rceil]:C_{m}\lesssim 2^{m}G+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)\cdot C_{m+1}}+\log(1/\delta)\}\,,

(A.19)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}

\displaystyle:=\{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{k}}+\log(1/\delta)\}\,,

(A.20)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{5}

\displaystyle:=\{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\sum_{h=1}^{H}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})-\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}V^{\pi^{k}}_{0;P^{*}}\lesssim\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{k}}\log(1/\delta)}+\log(1/\delta)\}\,,

(A.21)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{E}_{2}\cap\mathcal{E}_{3}\cap\mathcal{E}_{4}\cap\mathcal{E}_{5}\,.

(A.22)

First, by the realizability assumption, the standard generalization bound for MLE (
Lemma
A.1.7
) with simply setting
D_{i}
to be the delta distribution on the realized
(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})
pairs, and a union bound over
K
episodes, we have that w.p. at least
1-\delta
, for any
k\in[0,K-1]
:

(1)

P^{\star}\in\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{k}
;

(2)

\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{H}^{2}(P^{\star}(s_{h}^{i},a_{h}^{i})||\widehat{P}^{k}(s_{h}^{i},a_{h}^{i}))\leq 22\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta).

(A.23)

This directly indicates that

P(\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}_{1}\})\geq 1-\delta\,.

(A.24)

Under event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, with the realizability in above (1), and by the optimistic algorithm design
(\pi^{k},\widehat{P}^{k})\leftarrow\argmax_{\pi\in\Pi,P\in\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{k}}V_{0;P}^{\pi}(s_{0})
, for any
k\in[0,K-1]
, we have the following optimism guarantee

\displaystyle V^{\star}_{0;P^{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\pi\in\Pi,P\in\widehat{\mathcal{P}}^{k}}V^{\pi}_{0;P}=V^{\pi^{k}}_{0;\widehat{P}^{k}}.

Then, under event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, we use
Lemma
A.1.4
and
Equation
A.23
to get the following:

There exists a set
\mathcal{K}\subseteq[K-1]
such that

•

|\mathcal{K}|\leq 13\log^{2}(88\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)KH)\cdot DE_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/(176\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)KH))

•

And

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\mathrel{\|}\widehat{P}^{k}\big(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot(2+154\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH))+1

\displaystyle\lesssim\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)\,.

(A.25)

We upper bound the regret with optimism, and by dividing
k\in[K-1]
into
\mathcal{K}
and
[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}
with the assumption that the trajectory-wise cumulative reward is normalized in [0,1], as follows

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}V^{\star}_{0;P^{\star}}-\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\sum_{h=1}^{H}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})

\displaystyle\leq|\mathcal{K}|+\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\left(V^{\pi^{k}}_{0;\widehat{P}^{k}}-\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}(\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)KH)\cdot DE_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/(\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)KH))

\displaystyle+\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\left(V^{\pi^{k}}_{0;\widehat{P}^{k}}-\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right)\,.

(A.26)

We then do the following decomposition. Note that for any
k\in[K-1]
, policy
\pi^{k}
is deterministic. We have that for any
k\in[K-1]

\displaystyle V^{\pi^{k}}_{0;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s_{0}^{k})-\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})

\displaystyle=Q^{\pi^{k}}_{0;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s_{0}^{k},a_{0}^{k})-\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})

\displaystyle=r(s_{0}^{k},a_{0}^{k})+\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}^{k}(s_{0}^{k},a_{0}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})-\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}^{k}(s_{0}^{k},a_{0}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})-\sum_{h=1}^{H}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{*}(s_{0}^{k},a_{0}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})-\sum_{h=1}^{H}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})+\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}^{k}(s_{0}^{k},a_{0}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{*}(s_{0}^{k},a_{0}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})

\displaystyle=V^{\pi^{k}}_{1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s_{1}^{k})-\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{*}(s_{0}^{k},a_{0}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})-V^{\pi^{k}}_{1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s_{1}^{k})}_{I_{0}^{k}}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt+\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}^{k}(s_{0}^{k},a_{0}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{*}(s_{0}^{k},a_{0}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})\,,

where we use the Bellman equation for several times.

Then, by doing this recursively, we can get for any
k\in[K-1]

\displaystyle V^{\pi^{k}}_{0;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s_{h}^{k})-\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}I_{h}^{k}+\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left|\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}^{k}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{*}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{{h+1};\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})\right|

(A.27)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}(V^{\pi^{k}}_{0;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s_{h}^{k})-\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k}))

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}I_{h}^{k}+\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left|\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}^{k}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{*}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{{h+1};\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})\right|

(A.28)

Next we bound
\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}I_{h}^{k}
. Note that by Azuma Bernstein’s inequality, with probability at least
1-\delta

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}I_{h}^{k}\leq\sqrt{2\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\log(1/\delta)}+\frac{2}{3}\log(1/\delta)

(A.29)

This directly indicates that

P(\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}_{2}\})\geq 1-\delta\,.

(A.30)

Then, we propose the following lemma.

Lemma A.1.10

(Bound of sum of mean value differences for online RL)
.

Under event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left|\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}^{k}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{*}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{{h+1};\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]\cdot\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH).

Proof
[Proof of
Lemma
A.1.10
]
Under event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left|\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}^{k}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{*}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{{h+1};\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\sqrt{\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})D_{\triangle}\Big(V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big(s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\big)\mathrel{\|}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}(s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}^{k}\big(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\big)\Big)}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+5\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[D_{\triangle}\Big(V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big(s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\big)\mathrel{\|}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}(s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}^{k}\big(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\big)\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\sqrt{\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big(s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\big)\mathrel{\|}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}(s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}^{k}\big(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\big)\Big)}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+20\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big(s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\big)\mathrel{\|}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}(s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}^{k}\big(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\big)\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\sqrt{\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\mathrel{\|}\widehat{P}^{k}\big(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\Big)}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+20\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\mathrel{\|}\widehat{P}^{k}\big(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8\sqrt{\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]\cdot\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\mathrel{\|}\widehat{P}^{k}\big(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\Big)\right]}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+20\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\mathrel{\|}\widehat{P}^{k}\big(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]\cdot\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)\,,

(A.31)

where in the first inequality, we use
Lemma
3.2.1
to bound the difference of two means
\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}^{k}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}V^{\pi^{*}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})
using variances and the triangle discrimination; in the second inequality we use the fact that that triangle discrimination is equivalent to squared Hellinger distance, i.e.,
D_{\triangle}\leq 4\mathbb{H}^{2}
; the third inequality is via data processing inequality on the squared Hellinger distance; the fourth inequality is by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality; the last inequality holds under
\mathcal{E}_{1}
by
Equation
A.25
.

The next lemma shows that the event
\mathcal{E}_{3}
happens with high probability.

Lemma A.1.11

(Recursion Event Lemma)
.

Event
\mathcal{E}_{3}
happens with high probability. Specifically, we have

P(\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}_{3}\})\geq 1-(1+\lceil\log_{2}(\frac{KH}{G})\rceil)\delta.

(A.32)

Proof
[Proof of
Lemma
A.1.11
]
Let
\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}:=V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}-V_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}
. First, under event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, with happens with probability at least
1-\delta
by
Equation
A.24
, and also note that
\pi^{k}
is deterministic for any
k\in[K-1]
, we can prove the following

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\left|(\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{k}})(s_{h}^{k})-\big(P^{\star}\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right|\right]

(A.33)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\left|({V}_{h;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}})(s_{h}^{k})-\big(P^{\star}{V}_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})-\Big(({V}_{h}^{\pi^{k}})(s_{h}^{k})-\big(P^{\star}{V}_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\Big)\right|\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\left|r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})+\big(\widehat{P}^{k}{V}_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})-\big(P^{\star}{V}_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})-r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right|\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\left|\big(\widehat{P}^{k}{V}_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})-\big(P^{\star}{V}_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right|\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\left|\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}\left[V^{\pi^{k}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})\right]-\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}^{k}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}\left[V^{\pi^{k}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})\right]\right|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]\cdot\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)

\displaystyle=G

(A.34)

where the first equality is by the definition of
\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}
, the inequality holds under
\mathcal{E}_{1}
by
Lemma
A.1.10
, and the last equality is by definition of
A
and
G
.

Under event
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, with probability at least
1-\lceil\log_{2}(\frac{KH}{G})\rceil\delta
, for any
m\in[0,\lceil\log_{2}(\frac{KH}{G})\rceil]

\displaystyle C_{m}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}(V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}-V_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(P^{\star}(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m+1}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})-\big((P^{\star}(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}})(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m+1}}(s_{h+1}^{k})\right]-\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big((P^{\star}(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}})(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{s\sim P^{*}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}\left[(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m+1}}(s)\right]-(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m+1}}(s_{h+1}^{k})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[(\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m+1}}(s_{h}^{k})-\big((P^{\star}(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}})(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\big)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left(\mathbb{E}_{s\sim P^{*}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})}\left[(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m+1}}(s)\right]-(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m+1}}(s_{h}^{k})\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[(\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m+1}}(s_{h}^{k})-\big((P^{\star}(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}})(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\big)^{2}\right]+\log(1/\delta)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sqrt{\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{V}_{P^{*}}\left((V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}-V_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m+1}}\right)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\log(1/\delta)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\Big((\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}}(s_{h}^{k})+(P^{\star}(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}})(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\Big)\cdot\Big((\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}}(s_{h}^{k})-(P^{\star}(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}})(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)\cdot C_{m+1}}+\log(1/\delta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\Big((\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}}(s_{h}^{k})+(P^{\star}(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}})(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\Big)\cdot\Big((\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}}(s_{h}^{k})-(P^{\star}((\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2})^{2^{m-1}})(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)\cdot C_{m+1}}+\log(1/\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\Big((\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}}(s_{h}^{k})+(P^{\star}(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}})(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\Big)\cdot\Big((\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{k}})^{2^{m}}(s_{h}^{k})-((P^{\star}(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})^{2})(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k}))^{2^{m-1}}\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)\cdot C_{m+1}}+\log(1/\delta)

\displaystyle\leq 2^{m}\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\left|(\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{k}})^{2}(s_{h}^{k})-((P^{\star}\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k}))^{2}\right|\right]+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)\cdot C_{m+1}}+\log(1/\delta)

\displaystyle=2^{m}\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\left|\big((\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{k}})(s_{h}^{k})+(P^{\star}\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\big)\cdot\big((\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{k}})(s_{h}^{k})-(P^{\star}\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}})(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\big)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)\cdot C_{m+1}}+\log(1/\delta)

\displaystyle\leq 2\cdot 2^{m}\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\left|(\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{k}})(s_{h}^{k})-\big(P^{\star}\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right|\right]+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)\cdot C_{m+1}}+\log(1/\delta)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{m}G+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)\cdot C_{m+1}}+\log(1/\delta)\,,

(A.35)

where in the first inequality we change the index, the second inequality holds with probability at least
1-\delta
by Azuma Bernstain’s inequality, the third inequality holds because that
E[X^{2^{m-1}}]\geq(E[X])^{2^{m-1}}
for
m\geq 1
and
X\geq 0
, the fourth inequality holds by keep using
a^{2}-b^{2}=(a+b)(a-b)
, then with
E[X^{2}]\geq E[X]^{2}
, and the assumption that the trajectory-wise total reward is normalized in
[0,1]
, the last inequality holds under
\mathcal{E}_{1}
by
Equation
A.34
, and we take a union bound to get this hold for all
m\in[0,\lceil\log_{2}(\frac{KH}{G})\rceil]
with probability at least
1-\lceil\log_{2}(\frac{KH}{G})\rceil\delta
(because for each
m\in[0,\lceil\log_{2}(\frac{KH}{G})\rceil]
we need to apply the Azuma Bernstain’s inequality once).

The above reasoning directly implies that

P(\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}_{3}\})\geq 1-(1+\lceil\log_{2}(\frac{KH}{G})\rceil)\delta.

(A.36)

Under the event
\mathcal{E}_{3}
, we prove the following lemma to bound
\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]
.

Lemma A.1.12

(Variance Conversion Lemma for online RL)
.

Under event
\mathcal{E}_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]

\displaystyle\leq O\Big(\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]+\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)\Big).

Proof
[Proof of
Lemma
A.1.12
]
Under
\mathcal{E}_{3}
, we have for any
m\in[0,\lceil\log_{2}(\frac{KH}{G})\rceil]

C_{m}\lesssim 2^{m}G+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)\cdot C_{m+1}}+\log(1/\delta)\,.

(A.37)

Then, by
Lemma
A.1.9
, we have

C_{0}\lesssim G\,.

(A.38)

Also note that we have
A\leq 2B+2C_{0}
since
\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}(a+b)\leq 2\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}(a)+2\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}(b)
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\leq 2B+2C_{0}

\displaystyle\lesssim B+G

\displaystyle=B+\sqrt{A\cdot\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt+\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)

(A.39)

Then, with the fact that
x\leq 2a+b^{2}
if
x\leq a+b\sqrt{x}
, we have

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\leq O\Bigg(B+\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)\Bigg)\,,

(A.40)

which is

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]

\displaystyle\leq O\bigg(\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]+\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)\bigg)

(A.41)

By the same reasoning in Lemma 26 of
[
293
]
, we have that with probability at least
1-\delta

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]

\displaystyle\leq O(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{k}}+\log(1/\delta))\,.

(A.42)

This indicates that

P(\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}_{4}\})\geq 1-\delta\,.

(A.43)

We can use the Azuma Bernstain’s inequality to get that with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\sum_{h=1}^{H}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})-\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}V^{\pi^{k}}_{0;P^{*}}\lesssim\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{k}}\log(1/\delta)}+\log(1/\delta)\,.

(A.44)

This indicates that

P(\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}_{5}\})\geq 1-\delta\,.

(A.45)

Then, together with
Lemma
A.1.11
,
Equation
A.24
and
Equation
A.30
, we have

P(\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\})\geq 1-(5+\lceil\log_{2}(\frac{KH}{G})\rceil)\delta\geq 1-5KH\delta\,.

(A.46)

Finally, under event
\mathcal{E}
, with all the things above (
Equation
A.26
,
Equation
A.28
,
Equation
A.29
,
Lemma
A.1.10
,
Lemma
A.1.12
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}V^{\star}_{0;P^{\star}}-\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}V^{\pi^{k}}_{0;P^{*}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}V^{\star}_{0;P^{\star}}-\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\sum_{h=1}^{H}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})+\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\sum_{h=1}^{H}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})-\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}V^{\pi^{k}}_{0;P^{*}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|\mathcal{K}|+\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\left(V^{\pi^{k}}_{0;\widehat{P}^{k}}-\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right)+\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{k}}\log(1/\delta)}+\log(1/\delta)

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}(\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)KH)\cdot DE_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/(\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)KH))+\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\left(V^{\pi^{k}}_{0;\widehat{P}^{k}}-\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}r(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{k}}\log(1/\delta)}+\log(1/\delta)

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}(\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)KH)\cdot DE_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/(\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)KH))+\log(1/\delta)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sqrt{\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\log(1/\delta)}+\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{k}}\log(1/\delta)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left|\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}^{k}(s_{1}^{k},A^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{*}(s_{1}^{k},A^{k})}V^{\pi^{k}}_{1;\widehat{P}^{k}}(s^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}(\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)KH)\cdot DE_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/(\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)KH))+\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{k}}\log(1/\delta)}+\log(1/\delta)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{(\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]+\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH))\cdot\log(1/\delta)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sqrt{\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}^{k}}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]\cdot\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}(\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)KH)\cdot DE_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/(\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)KH))+\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{k}}\log(1/\delta)}+\log(1/\delta)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{(\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]+\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH))\cdot\log(1/\delta)}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{(\sum_{k\in[K-1]\setminus\mathcal{K}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right]+\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH))}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\times\sqrt{\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH))}

\displaystyle\lesssim\log^{2}(\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)KH)\cdot DE_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/(\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)KH))+\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{k}}\log(1/\delta)}+\log(1/\delta)

\displaystyle+\sqrt{(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{k}}+\log(1/\delta)+\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH))\cdot\log(1/\delta)}

\displaystyle+\sqrt{\left(\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{k}}+\log\left(\frac{1}{\delta}\right)+\text{DE}_{1}\left(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},\frac{1}{KH}\right)\cdot\log\left(\frac{K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|}{\delta}\right)\log(KH)\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\times\sqrt{\text{DE}_{1}\left(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},\frac{1}{KH}\right)\log\left(\frac{K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|}{\delta}\right)\log(KH)}

\displaystyle\leq O\Big(\sqrt{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{k}}\cdot\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\text{DE}_{1}(\Psi,{\mathcal{S}}\times\mathcal{A},1/KH)\cdot\log(K\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)\log(KH)\Big)\,.

(A.47)

The final result follows by replacing
\delta
to be
\delta/(5KH)
to make the event
\mathcal{E}
happen with probability at least
1-\delta
.

A.1.6
Proof of
Corollary
3.2

Proof
[Proof of
Corollary
3.2
]
By
Lemma
A.1.6
, we have

\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{k}}=\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{k}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s,a)\right]

(A.48)

Therefore, when
P^{\star}
is deterministic,
the
\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{k}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{k}}\big)(s,a)\right]
terms are all 0 for any
k\in[K-1]
and
h\in[H-1]
, and then the
\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{k}}
term in the higher order term in
Theorem
3.3.2
is 0.

A.1.7
Proof of
Corollary
3.3

Proof
[Proof of
Corollary
3.3
]
We follow the MLE guarantee for the infinite model class in
Lemma
A.1.8
and the same proof steps in the proof of
Theorem
3.3.2
in Appendix
A.1.5
.

A.1.8
Detailed Proofs for the Offline RL setting in
Section
3.4

A.1.9
Proof of
Theorem
3.4.1

The following is the full proof of
Theorem
3.4.1
.

Proof
[Proof of
Theorem
3.4.1
]
First, by the realizability assumption, the standard generalization bound for MLE (
Lemma
A.1.7
) with simply setting
D_{i}
to be the delta distribution on the
(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})
pairs in the offline dataset
\mathcal{D}
, we have that w.p. at least
1-\delta
:

(1)

P^{\star}\in\widehat{\mathcal{P}}
;

(2)

\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{H}^{2}(P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})||\widehat{P}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k}))\leq\frac{22\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}.

(A.49)

Then, with the above realizability in (1), and by the pessimistic algorithm design
\hat{\pi}\leftarrow\argmax_{\pi\in\Pi}\min_{P\in\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}V_{0;P}^{\pi}(s_{0})
,
\widehat{P}\leftarrow\argmin_{P\in\widehat{\mathcal{P}}}V_{0;P}^{\widehat{\pi}}(s_{0})
, we have that for any
\pi^{\star}\in\Pi

\displaystyle V_{0;P^{\star}}^{\pi^{\star}}-V_{0;P^{\star}}^{\hat{\pi}}

\displaystyle=V_{0;P^{\star}}^{\pi^{\star}}-V_{0;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}+V_{0;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}-V_{0;P^{\star}}^{\hat{\pi}}

\displaystyle\leq V_{0;P^{\star}}^{\pi^{\star}}-V_{0;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}+V_{0;\widehat{P}}^{\hat{\pi}}-V_{0;P^{\star}}^{\hat{\pi}}

\displaystyle\leq V_{0;P^{\star}}^{\pi^{\star}}-V_{0;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\,.

(A.50)

We can then bound
V_{0;P^{\star}}^{\pi^{\star}}-V_{0;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}
using the simulation lemma (
Lemma
A.1.5
):

\displaystyle V_{0;P^{\star}}^{\pi^{\star}}-V_{0;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\left|\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s,a)}V^{\pi^{\star}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}(s^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(s,a)}V^{\pi^{\star}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}(s^{\prime})\right|\right]\,.

(A.51)

Then, we prove the following lemma to bound the RHS of
Equation
A.51
.

Lemma A.1.13

(Bound of sum of mean value differences for offline RL)
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\left|\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s,a)}V^{\pi^{\star}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}(s^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(s,a)}V^{\pi^{\star}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}(s^{\prime})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8\sqrt{\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\right]\cdot\frac{22C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}}+\frac{440C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}.

Proof
[Proof of
Lemma
A.1.13
]
We have

\displaystyle\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\left|\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s,a)}V^{\pi^{\star}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}(s^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(s,a)}V^{\pi^{\star}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}(s^{\prime})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\sqrt{\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)D_{\triangle}\Big(V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big(s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s,a)\big)\mathrel{\|}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}(s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}\big(s,a)\big)\Big)}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+5\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[D_{\triangle}\Big(V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big(s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s,a)\big)\mathrel{\|}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}(s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}\big(s,a)\big)\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\sqrt{\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big(s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s,a)\big)\mathrel{\|}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}(s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}\big(s,a)\big)\Big)}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+20\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big(s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s,a)\big)\mathrel{\|}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}(s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}\big(s,a)\big)\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\sqrt{\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(P^{\star}(s,a)\mathrel{\|}\widehat{P}\big(s,a)\Big)}\right]+20\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(P^{\star}(s,a)\mathrel{\|}\widehat{P}\big(s,a)\Big)\right]

(A.52)

where in the first inequality, we use
Lemma
3.2.1
to bound the difference of two means
\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s,a)}V^{\pi^{\star}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}(s^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(s,a)}V^{\pi^{*}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}(s^{\prime})
using variances and the triangle discrimination; in the second inequality we use the fact that that triangle discrimination is equivalent to squared Hellinger distance, i.e.,
D_{\triangle}\leq 4\mathbb{H}^{2}
; the third inequality is via data processing inequality on the squared Hellinger distance. Next, starting from
Equation
A.52
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle 8\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\sqrt{\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(P^{\star}(s,a)\mathrel{\|}\widehat{P}\big(s,a)\Big)}\right]+20\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(P^{\star}(s,a)\mathrel{\|}\widehat{P}\big(s,a)\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8\sqrt{\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\right]\cdot\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(P^{\star}(s,a)\mathrel{\|}\widehat{P}\big(s,a)\Big)\right]}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+20\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\mathbb{H}^{2}\Big(P^{\star}(s,a)\mathrel{\|}\widehat{P}\big(s,a)\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8\sqrt{\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\right]\cdot C^{\pi^{\star}}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{H}^{2}(P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})||\widehat{P}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k}))}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+20C^{\pi^{\star}}\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{H}^{2}(P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})||\widehat{P}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k}))

\displaystyle\leq 8\sqrt{\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\right]\cdot\frac{22C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}}+\frac{440C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}\,,

(A.53)

where the first inequality is by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality; the second inequality is by the definition of single policy coverage (
Definition
3.3
); the last inequality holds with probability at least
1-\delta
with
Equation
A.49
. Substituting
Equation
A.53
into
Equation
A.52
ends our proof.

We denote
\mathcal{E}
as the event that
Lemma
A.1.13
holds. Under the event
\mathcal{E}
, we prove the following lemma to bound
\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\right]
with
\widetilde{O}(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{*}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{*}}\big)(s,a)\right]+C^{\pi^{*}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)/{K}
).

Lemma A.1.14

(Variance Conversion Lemma for offline RL)
.

Under event
\mathcal{E}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\right]\leq O\Big(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{*}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{*}}\big)(s,a)\right]+C^{\pi^{*}}\frac{\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}\Big).

Proof
[Proof of
Lemma
A.1.14
]
For notational simplicity, we denote
A:=\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\right]
, and we denote

B:=\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\right]
,

C:=\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}(V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}-V_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}})\big)(s,a)\right]
, then we have

\displaystyle A\leq 2B+2C,

since
\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}(a+b)\leq 2\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}(a)+2\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}(b)
.

Let
\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}}:=V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}-V_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}}
. Then, w.p. at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\big(P^{\star}(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}})^{2}\big)(s,a)-\big(P^{\star}\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)^{2}(s,a)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h+1}}\left[(\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}})^{2}(s)\right]-\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\big(P^{\star}\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)^{2}(s,a)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[(\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{\star}})^{2}(s)-\big(P^{\star}\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)^{2}(s,a)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\Big((\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{\star}})(s)+\big(P^{\star}\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\Big)\cdot\Big((\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{\star}})(s)-\big(P^{\star}\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\Big)\right],

(A.54)

where the first equality is by the definition of variance, the second equality holds as
d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}
is the occupancy measure also generated under
P^{\star}
, the first inequality is just changing the index, the third equality holds as
a^{2}-b^{2}=(a+b)\cdot(a-b)
. Starting from
Equation
A.54
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\Big((\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{\star}})(s)+\big(P^{\star}\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\Big)\cdot\Big((\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{\star}})(s)-\big(P^{\star}\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\left|(\Delta_{h}^{\pi^{\star}})(s)-\big(P^{\star}\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\right|\right]

\displaystyle=2\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\left|({V}_{h;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}})(s)-\big(P^{\star}{V}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)-\Big(({V}_{h}^{\pi^{\star}})(s)-\big(P^{\star}{V}_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\Big)\right|\right]

\displaystyle=2\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\left|r(s,a)+\big(\widehat{P}{V}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)-\big(P^{\star}{V}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)-r(s,a)\right|\right]

\displaystyle=2\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\left|\big(\widehat{P}{V}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)-\big(P^{\star}{V}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\right|\right],

(A.55)

where the inequality holds as the value functions are all bounded by 1 by the assumption that the total reward over any trajectory is bounded by 1, the first equality is by the definition of
\Delta_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}}
, the second equality is because
a
is drawn from
\pi^{\star}
. Starting from
Equation
A.55
, we have

\displaystyle 2\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\left|\big(\widehat{P}{V}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)-\big(P^{\star}{V}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\right|\right]

\displaystyle=2\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\left|\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim P^{\star}(s,a)}\left[V^{\pi^{\star}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}(s^{\prime})\right]-\mathbb{E}_{s^{\prime}\sim\widehat{P}(\cdot|s,a)}\left[V^{\pi^{\star}}_{h+1;\widehat{P}}(s^{\prime})\right]\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 16\sqrt{\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1;\widehat{P}}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\right]\cdot\frac{22C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}}+\frac{880C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}

\displaystyle=16\sqrt{A\cdot\frac{22C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}}+\frac{880C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}

(A.56)

where the inequality holds with probability at least
1-\delta
by
Lemma
A.1.13
, and the second equality is by definition of
A
.

Then combining
Equation
A.54
,
Equation
A.55
and
Equation
A.56
, we obtain an upper bound for
C
, which suggests

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\leq 2B+2C

\displaystyle\leq 2B+\frac{1760C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}+32\sqrt{\frac{22C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}}\cdot\sqrt{A}.

Then, with the fact that
x\leq 2a+b^{2}
if
x\leq a+b\sqrt{x}
, we have

\displaystyle A\leq 4B+\frac{3520C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}+\frac{22528C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}\leq O(B+\frac{C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}).

With the above lemmas, we can now prove the final results of
Theorem
3.4.1
. We have that w.p. at least
1-\delta

\displaystyle V_{0;P^{\star}}^{\pi^{\star}}-V_{0;P^{\star}}^{\hat{\pi}}

\displaystyle\leq O\Big(\sqrt{A\cdot\frac{C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}}+\frac{C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq O\Big(\sqrt{(B+\frac{C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K})\cdot\frac{C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}}+\frac{C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq O\Big(\sqrt{B\cdot\frac{C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}}+\sqrt{\frac{C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}\cdot\frac{C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}}+\frac{C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}\Big)

\displaystyle=O\Big(\sqrt{\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{\star}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{\star}}\big)(s,a)\right]\cdot\frac{C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}}+\frac{C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}\Big)

(A.57)

\displaystyle=O\Big(\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{\star}}C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}}+\frac{C^{\pi^{\star}}\log(\left|\mathcal{P}\right|/\delta)}{K}\Big)\,,

where in the last equation we use
Lemma
A.1.6
, and
\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{\star}}:=\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}r(s_{h},\pi^{\star}(s_{h}))-V_{0}^{\pi^{\star}}\bigg)^{2}\right]
.

A.1.10
Proof of
Corollary
3.4

Proof
[Proof of
Corollary
3.4
]
By
Lemma
A.1.6
, we have

\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{*}}=\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{*}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{*}}\big)(s,a)\right]

(A.58)

Therefore, when
P^{\star}
is deterministic,
the
\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{*}}_{h}}\left[\big(\mathbb{V}_{P^{\star}}V_{h+1}^{\pi^{*}}\big)(s,a)\right]
terms are all 0 for any
k\in[K-1]
and
h\in[H-1]
, and then the
\mathrm{VaR}_{\pi^{*}}
term in the higher order term in
Theorem
3.4.1
is 0.

A.1.11
Proof of
Corollary
3.5

Proof
[Proof of
Corollary
3.5
]
This claim follows the proof of
Theorem
3.4.1
, while we take a different choice of
\beta
that depends on the bracketing number and follow the MLE guarantee in
Lemma
A.1.8
for infinite model class.

A.1.12
Proof of the claim in
Example
2

Proof

Recall that in
Definition
3.3
, we have

\displaystyle C^{\pi^{*}}_{\mathcal{D}}:=\max_{h,P\in\mathcal{P}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim d^{\pi^{*}}_{h}}\mathbb{H}^{2}\left(P(s,a)\mathrel{\|}P^{\star}(s,a)\right)}{1/K\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{H}^{2}\left(P(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\mathrel{\|}P^{\star}(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\right)}\,.

For each step
h
, define two distributions,
p_{h},q_{h}
, where
p_{h}(s,a)=d^{\pi^{*}}(s,a)
,
q_{h}(s,a)=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{I}\{(s,a)=(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\}
, and we define
f(s,a,P)=\mathbb{H}^{2}(P(s,a)\mathrel{\|}P^{\star}(s,a))
, then we have

\displaystyle C^{\pi^{*}}_{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle=\max_{h,P\in\mathcal{P}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim p_{h}}f(s,a,P)}{\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim q_{h}}f(s,a,P)}

\displaystyle=\max_{h,P\in\mathcal{P}}\frac{\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim q_{h}}\frac{p_{h}(s,a)}{q_{h}(s,a)}f(s,a,P)}{\mathbb{E}_{s,a\sim q_{h}}f(s,a,P)}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{h,s,a}\frac{p_{h}(s,a)}{q_{h}(s,a)}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{h,s,a}\frac{1}{q_{h}(s,a)}\,.

(A.59)

Note that for all
h
,
\{(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})\}_{k=1}^{K}
are i.i.d. samples drawn from
d_{h}^{\pi^{b}}
, therefore,
\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{I}\{(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})=(s,a)\}]=d^{\pi^{b}}_{h}(s,a)
. By Hoeffding’s inequality and with a union bound over
s,a,h
, and for
K\geq\frac{2\log(\frac{|{\mathcal{S}}||\mathcal{A}|H}{\delta})}{\rho_{\min}^{2}}
, w.p. at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle q_{h}(s,a)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathbb{I}\{(s_{h}^{k},a_{h}^{k})=(s,a)\}

\displaystyle\geq d^{\pi^{b}}_{h}(s,a)-\sqrt{\frac{\log(\frac{|{\mathcal{S}}||\mathcal{A}|H}{\delta})}{2K}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{d^{\pi^{b}}_{h}(s,a)}{2}\,,

(A.60)

where in the last inequality we use the assumption that
d^{\pi^{b}}_{h}(s,a)\geq\rho_{\min},\forall s,a,h
, which gives us
K\geq\frac{2\log(\frac{|{\mathcal{S}}||\mathcal{A}|H}{\delta})}{\rho_{\min}^{2}}\geq\max_{s,a,h}\frac{2\log(\frac{|{\mathcal{S}}||\mathcal{A}|H}{\delta})}{(d^{\pi^{b}}_{h}(s,a))^{2}}
, so
K\geq\frac{2\log(\frac{|{\mathcal{S}}||\mathcal{A}|H}{\delta})}{(d^{\pi^{b}}_{h}(s,a))^{2}}
for any
s,a,h
.

Therefore, with
K\geq\frac{2\log(\frac{|{\mathcal{S}}||\mathcal{A}|H}{\delta})}{\rho_{\min}^{2}}
, we have that w.p. at least
1-\delta

\displaystyle C^{\pi^{*}}_{\mathcal{D}}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{h,s,a}\frac{1}{q_{h}(s,a)}\leq\max_{h,s,a}\frac{2}{d^{\pi^{b}}_{h}(s,a)}\leq\frac{2}{\rho_{min}}\,.

(A.61)

and

m
groups (
m<n

on linear MDPs. Namely, we provide proof of Theorem
4.5.1

4.1

A.2.1
Results in Section
4.3

A.2.1.1
Proof of Proposition
4.1

Let
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\{(x_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},a_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},r_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau})\}_{h=1,\tau=1}^{H,K}
denote the merged dataset, where each trajectory belongs to a context
c_{\tau}
. For simplicity, let
\mathcal{D}_{c}
denote the collection of trajectories that belong to MDP
\mathcal{M}_{c}
.
Then each trajectory in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is generated by the following steps:

•

The experimenter randomly samples an environment
c\sim C
.

•

The experimenter collect a trajectory from the episodic MDP
\mathcal{M}_{c}
.

Then for any
x^{\prime},r^{\prime},\tau
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(r_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau}=r^{\prime},x_{c_{\tau},h+1}^{\tau}=x^{\prime}|\{(x_{c_{j},h}^{j},a_{c_{j},h}^{j})\}_{j=1}^{\tau},\{r_{c_{j},h}^{j},x_{c_{j},h+1}^{j}\}_{j=1}^{\tau-1})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(r_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau}=r^{\prime},x_{c_{\tau},h+1}^{\tau}=x^{\prime},\{(x_{c_{j},h}^{j},a_{c_{j},h}^{j})\}_{j=1}^{\tau},\{r_{c_{j},h}^{j},x_{c_{j},h+1}^{j}\}_{j=1}^{\tau-1})}{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(\{(x_{c_{j},h}^{j},a_{c_{j},h}^{j})\}_{j=1}^{\tau},\{r_{c_{j},h}^{j},x_{c_{j},h+1}^{j}\}_{j=1}^{\tau-1})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{c\in C}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(r_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau}=r^{\prime},x_{c_{\tau},h+1}^{\tau}=x^{\prime}|\{(x_{c_{j},h}^{j},a_{c_{j},h}^{j})\}_{j=1}^{\tau},\{r_{c_{j},h}^{j},x_{c_{j},h+1}^{j}\}_{j=1}^{\tau-1},c_{\tau}=c)q(c),

(A.62)

where

\displaystyle q(c^{\prime}):=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(\{(x_{c_{j},h}^{j},a_{c_{j},h}^{j})\}_{j=1}^{\tau},\{r_{c_{j},h}^{j},x_{c_{j},h+1}^{j}\}_{j=1}^{\tau-1},c_{\tau}=c^{\prime})}{\sum_{c\in C}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(\{(x_{c_{j},h}^{j},a_{c_{j},h}^{j})\}_{j=1}^{\tau},\{r_{c_{j},h}^{j},x_{c_{j},h+1}^{j}\}_{j=1}^{\tau-1},c_{\tau}=c)}.

Next, we further have

(
A.62
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{c\in C}\mathbb{P}_{c}(r_{c,h}(s_{h})=r^{\prime},s_{h+1}=x^{\prime}|s_{h}=x_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},a_{h}=a_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau})q(c)

\displaystyle=\sum_{c\in C}\frac{\mathbb{P}_{c}(r_{c,h}(s_{h})=r^{\prime},s_{h+1}=x^{\prime}|s_{h}=x_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},a_{h}=a_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau})\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s_{h}=x_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},a_{h}=a_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},c_{\tau}=c)}{\sum_{c\in C}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}(s_{h}=x_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},a_{h}=a_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},c_{\tau}=c)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{c\in C}p(c)\cdot\frac{\mathbb{P}_{c}(r_{c,h}(s_{h})=r^{\prime},s_{h+1}=x^{\prime}|s_{h}=x_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},a_{h}=a_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau})\mathbb{P}_{c}(s_{h}=x_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},a_{h}=a_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau})}{\sum_{c\in C}p(c)\cdot\mathbb{P}_{c}(s_{h}=x_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},a_{h}=a_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau})}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}\frac{\mathbb{P}_{c}(r_{c,h}(s_{h})=r^{\prime},s_{h+1}=x^{\prime}|s_{h}=x_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},a_{h}=a_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau})\mu_{c,h}(x_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},a_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau})}{\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}\mu_{c,h}(x_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},a_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau})},

where the first equality holds since for all trajectories
\tau
satisfying
c_{\tau}=c
, they are compliant with
\mathcal{M}_{c}
, the second one holds since all trajectories are independent of each other, the third and fourth ones hold due to the definition of
\mu_{c,h}(\cdot,\cdot)
.

A.2.1.2
PEVI algorithm

Algorithm 14

[
110
]
Pessimistic Value Iteration (PEVI)

0:

Dataset
\mathcal{D}=\{(x_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},a_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau},r_{c_{\tau},h}^{\tau})_{h=1}^{H}\}_{\tau=1}^{K}
, confidence probability
\delta\in(0,1)
.

1:

Initialization: Set
\hat{V}_{H+1}(\cdot)\leftarrow 0
.

2:

for
step
h=H,H-1,\ldots,1

do

3:

Set
\Lambda_{h}\leftarrow\sum_{\tau=1}^{K}\phi(x_{h}^{\tau},a_{h}^{\tau})\phi(x_{h}^{\tau},a_{h}^{\tau})^{\top}+\lambda\cdot I
.

4:

Set
\hat{w}_{h}\leftarrow\Lambda_{h}^{-1}(\sum_{\tau=1}^{K}\phi(x_{h}^{\tau},a_{h}^{\tau})\cdot(r_{h}^{\tau}+\hat{V}_{h+1}(x_{h+1}^{\tau})))
.

5:

Set
\Gamma_{h}(\cdot,\cdot)\leftarrow\beta(\delta)\cdot(\phi(\cdot,\cdot)^{\top}\Lambda_{h}^{-1}\phi(\cdot,\cdot))^{1/2}
.

6:

Set
\hat{Q}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot)\leftarrow\min\{\phi(\cdot,\cdot)^{\top}\hat{w}_{h}-\Gamma_{h}(\cdot,\cdot),H-h+1\}^{+}
.

7:

Set
\hat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\cdot)\leftarrow\argmax_{\pi_{h}}\langle\hat{Q}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot),\pi_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\cdot)\rangle_{\mathcal{A}}
.

8:

Set
\hat{V}_{h}(\cdot)\leftarrow\langle\hat{Q}_{h}(\cdot,\cdot),\hat{\pi}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}\cdot)\rangle_{\mathcal{A}}
.

9:

end

for

10:

return

\pi^{\text{PEVI}}=\{\hat{\pi}_{h}\}_{h=1}^{H}
.

We analyze the suboptimality gap of the Pessimistic Value Iteration (PEVI) (
[
110
]
) in the contextual linear MDP setting without context information to demonstrate that by finding the optimal policy for
\bar{\mathcal{M}}
is not enough to find the policy that performs well on MDPs with context information.

Pessimistic Value Iteration (PEVI)
.
Let
\overline{\pi}^{*}
be the optimal policy w.r.t. the average MDP
\bar{\mathcal{M}}
. We analyze the performance of the Pessimistic Value Iteration (PEVI)
[
110
]
under the unknown context information setting. The details of PEVI is in Algo.
14
.

Suppose that
\bar{\mathcal{D}}
consists of
K
number of trajectories generated i.i.d. following by a fixed behavior policy
\bar{\pi}
. Then the following theorem shows the suboptimality gap for Algo.
14
does not converge to 0 even when the data size grows to infinity.

Theorem A.2.1
.

Assume that
\bar{\pi}

In Algo.
6
, we set

\lambda=1,\hskip 11.74988pt\beta(\delta)=c^{\prime}\cdot dH\sqrt{\log(4dHK/\delta)}\,,

(A.63)

where
c^{\prime}>0
is a positive constant.
Suppose we have

K\geq\tilde{c}\cdot d\log(4dH/\xi)
, where
\tilde{c}>0
is a sufficiently large positive constant that depends on
c
. Then we have: w.p. at least
1-\delta
, for the output policy
\pi^{\text{PEVI}}
of Algo.
14
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\pi}V_{\bar{\mathcal{M}},1}^{\pi}-V_{\bar{\mathcal{M}},1}^{\pi^{\text{PEVI}}}\leq c^{\prime\prime}\cdot d^{3/2}H^{2}K^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log(4dHK/\delta)},

(A.64)

and the suboptimality gap satisfies

\displaystyle\text{SubOpt}(\pi^{\text{PEVI}})

\displaystyle\leq c^{\prime\prime}\cdot d^{3/2}H^{2}K^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log(4dHK/\delta)}

\displaystyle+2\sup_{\pi}|V_{\bar{\mathcal{M}},1}^{\pi}(x_{1})-\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}V_{c,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})|\,,

(A.65)

where
c^{\prime\prime}>0
is a positive constant that only depends on
c
and
c^{\prime}
.

Proof of Theorem
A.2.1
.

First, we define the value function on the average MDP
\bar{\mathcal{M}}
as follows.

\overline{V}^{\pi}_{h}(x)=\mathbb{E}_{\pi,\bar{\mathcal{M}}}\Big[\sum_{i=h}^{H}r_{i}(s_{i},a_{i})\,\big|\,s_{h}=x\Big]\,.

(A.66)

We then decompose the suboptimality gap as follows.

\displaystyle\text{SubOpt}(\pi^{\text{PEVI}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}\big[V_{c,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})\big]-\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}\big[V_{c,1}^{\pi^{\text{PEVI}}}(x_{1})\big]

\displaystyle=\overline{V}^{\overline{\pi}^{*}}_{1}(x_{1})-\overline{V}^{\pi^{\text{PEVI}}}_{1}(x_{1})+\big(\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}\big[V_{c,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})\big]-\overline{V}^{\overline{\pi}^{*}}_{1}(x_{1})\big)

\displaystyle+\big(\overline{V}^{\pi^{\text{PEVI}}}_{1}(x_{1})-\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}\big[V_{c,1}^{\pi^{\text{PEVI}}}(x_{1})\big]\big)

\displaystyle\leq\overline{V}^{\overline{\pi}^{*}}_{1}(x_{1})-\overline{V}^{\pi^{\text{PEVI}}}_{1}(x_{1})+2\sup_{\pi}|V_{\bar{\mathcal{M}},1}^{\pi}(x_{1})-\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}V_{c,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})|\,.

(A.67)

Then, applying Corollary 4.6 in
[
110
]
, we can get that w.p. at least
1-\delta

\overline{V}^{\overline{\pi}^{*}}_{1}(x_{1})-\overline{V}^{\pi^{\text{PEVI}}}_{1}(x_{1})\leq c^{\prime\prime}\cdot d^{3/2}H^{2}K^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log(4dHK/\delta)}\,,

(A.68)

which, together with Eq.(
A.67
) completes the proof.

∎

Theorem
A.2.1
shows that by adapting the standard pessimistic offline RL algorithm over the offline dataset without context information, the learned policy
\pi^{\text{PEVI}}
converges to the optimal policy
\bar{\pi}^{*}
over the average MDP
\bar{\mathcal{M}}
.

A.2.2
Proof of Theorems in Section
4.4

A.2.2.1
Proof of Theorem
4.4.1

We define the model estimation error as

\iota_{i,h}^{\pi}(x,a)=(\mathbb{B}_{i,h}\hat{V}^{\pi}_{i,h+1})(x,a)-\hat{Q}^{\pi}_{i,h}(x,a).

(A.69)

And we define the following condition

\displaystyle\big|(\hat{\mathbb{B}}_{i,h}\hat{V}^{\pi}_{i,h+1})(x,a)-(\mathbb{B}_{i,h}\hat{V}^{\pi}_{i,h+1})(x,a)\big|\leq\Gamma_{i,h}(x,a)

\displaystyle\text{for all}~i\in[n],\pi\in\Pi,(x,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A},h\in[H]\,.

(A.70)

We introduce the following lemma to bound the model estimation error.

Lemma A.2.2

(Model estimation error bound (Adapted from Lemma 5.1 in
[
110
]
)
.

Under the condition of Eq.(
A.70
), we have

0\leq\iota_{i,h}^{\pi}(x,a)\leq 2\Gamma_{i,h}(x,a),\hskip 11.74988pt\text{for all}~~i\in[n],~\pi\in\Pi,~(x,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A},~h\in[H].

(A.71)

Then, we prove the following lemma for pessimism in V values.

Lemma A.2.3

(Pessimism for Estimated V Values)
.

Under the condition of Eq.(
A.70
), for any
i\in[n],\pi\in\Pi,x\in\mathcal{S}
, we have

V_{i,h}^{\pi}(x)\geq\hat{V}_{i,h}^{\pi}(x)\,.

(A.72)

Proof.

For any
i\in[n],\pi\in\Pi,x\in\mathcal{S},a\in\mathcal{A}
, we have

\displaystyle Q_{i,h}^{\pi}(x,a)-\hat{Q}_{i,h}^{\pi}(x,a)

\displaystyle\geq r_{i,h}(x,a)+(\mathbb{B}_{i,h}V^{\pi}_{i,h+1})(x,a)-\big(r_{i,h}(s,a)+(\hat{\mathbb{B}}_{i,h}\hat{V}^{\pi}_{i,h+1})(x,a)-\Gamma_{i,h}(x,a)\big)

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{B}_{i,h}V^{\pi}_{i,h+1})(x,a)-({\mathbb{B}}_{i,h}\hat{V}^{\pi}_{i,h+1})(x,a)+\Gamma_{i,h}(x,a)

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt-\big((\hat{\mathbb{B}}_{i,h}\hat{V}^{\pi}_{i,h+1})(x,a)-{\mathbb{B}}_{i,h}\hat{V}^{\pi}_{i,h+1})(x,a)\big)

\displaystyle\geq(\mathbb{B}_{i,h}V^{\pi}_{i,h+1})(x,a)-({\mathbb{B}}_{i,h}\hat{V}^{\pi}_{i,h+1})(x,a)

\displaystyle=\big(P_{i,h}(V_{i,h+1}^{\pi}-\hat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\pi})\big)(x,a)\,,

where the second inequality is because of Eq.(
A.70
). And since in the
H+1
step we have
V_{i,H+1}^{\pi}=\hat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\pi}=0
, we can get
Q_{i,H}^{\pi}(x,a)-\hat{Q}_{i,H}^{\pi}(x,a)
. Then we use induction to prove
Q_{i,h}^{\pi}(x,a)\geq\hat{Q}_{i,h}^{\pi}(x,a)
for all
h
. Given
Q_{i,h+1}^{\pi}(x,a)\geq\hat{Q}_{i,h+1}^{\pi}(x,a)
, we have

\displaystyle Q_{i,h}^{\pi}(x,a)-\hat{Q}_{i,h}^{\pi}(x,a)

\displaystyle\geq\big(P_{i,h}(V_{i,h+1}^{\pi}-\hat{V}_{i,h+1}^{\pi})\big)(x,a)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}{}{\langle Q_{i,h+1}^{\pi}(s_{h+1},\cdot)-\hat{Q}_{i,h+1}^{\pi}(s_{h+1},\cdot),\pi_{h+1}(\cdot|s_{h+1})\rangle_{\mathcal{A}}|s_{h}=x,a_{h}=a}

\displaystyle\geq 0\,.

(A.73)

Then we have

\displaystyle V_{i,h}^{\pi}(x)-\hat{V}_{i,h}^{\pi}(x)

\displaystyle=\langle Q_{i,h}^{\pi}(x,\cdot)-\hat{Q}_{i,h}^{\pi}(x,\cdot),\pi_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}x)\rangle_{\mathcal{A}}\geq 0\,.

∎

Then we start our proof.

Proof of Theorem
4.4.1
.

First, we decompose the suboptimality gap as follows

\displaystyle\text{SubOpt}(\pi^{\text{PERM}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}{V_{c,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-V_{c,1}^{\hat{\pi}^{*}}(x_{1})}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}{V_{c,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{\pi^{*}}_{i,1}(x_{1})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i,1}^{\pi^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})-\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}{V_{c,1}^{\pi^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(V_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-V_{i,1}^{\pi^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})\big)\,.

(A.74)

For the first two terms, we can bound them following the standard generalization techniques (
[
261
]
),
i.e.
, we use the covering argument, Chernoff bound,and union bound.

Define the distance between policies
d(\pi^{1},\pi^{2})\triangleq\max_{s\in\mathcal{S},h\in[H]}\|\pi^{1}_{h}(\cdot|s)-\pi^{2}_{h}(\cdot|s)\|_{1}
.
We construct the
\epsilon
-covering set
\tilde{\Pi}
w.r.t.
d
such that

\displaystyle\forall\pi\in\Pi,\exists\tilde{\pi}\in\tilde{\Pi},s.t.\hskip 11.74988ptd(\pi,\tilde{\pi})\leq\epsilon.

(A.75)

Then we have

\displaystyle\forall i\in[n],\pi\in\Pi,\exists\tilde{\pi}\in\tilde{\Pi},s.t.V_{i,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})-V_{i,1}^{\tilde{\pi}}(x_{1})\leq H\epsilon.

(A.76)

By the definition of the covering number,
\left|\tilde{\Pi}\right|=\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{\Pi}
. By Chernoff bound and union bound over the policy set
\tilde{\Pi}
, we have with prob. at least
1-\frac{\delta}{3}
, for any
\tilde{\pi}\in\tilde{\Pi}
,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{\tilde{\pi}}_{i,1}(x_{1})-\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}{V^{\tilde{\pi}}_{c,1}(x_{1})}\right|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\log(6\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{\Pi}/\delta)}{n}}.

(A.77)

By Eq.(
A.76
) and Eq.(
A.77
),
\forall i\in[n],\pi\in\Pi,\exists\tilde{\pi}\in\tilde{\Pi}
with
\left|\tilde{\Pi}\right|=\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{\Pi},~s.t.V_{i,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})-V_{i,1}^{\tilde{\pi}}(x_{1})\leq H\epsilon
, and with probability at least
1-\delta/3
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{{\pi}}_{i,1}(x_{1})-\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}{V^{{\pi}}_{c,1}(x_{1})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{\tilde{\pi}}_{i,1}(s_{1})-\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}{V^{{\tilde{\pi}}}_{c,1}(x_{1})}\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{{\pi}}_{i,1}(s_{1})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{\tilde{\pi}}_{i,1}(s_{1})\right|+\left|\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}{V^{{\tilde{\pi}}}_{c,1}(x_{1})}-\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}{V^{{\pi}}_{c,1}(x_{1})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\log(6\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{\Pi}/\delta)}{n}}+2H\epsilon\,.

(A.78)

Therefore, we have for the first two terms, w.p. at least
1-\frac{2}{3}\delta
we can upper bound them with
4H\epsilon+2\sqrt{\frac{2\log(6\mathcal{N}_{\epsilon}^{\Pi}/\delta)}{n}}
.

Then, what remains is to bound the term
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(V_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-V_{i,1}^{\pi^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})\big)
.

First, by similar arguments, we have

\displaystyle V_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-V_{i,1}^{\pi^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\big(V_{i,1}^{{\pi}^{*}}(x_{1})-V_{i,1}^{\tilde{\pi}^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})\big)+|V_{i,1}^{\tilde{\pi}^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})-V_{i,1}^{\pi^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})|

\displaystyle\leq H\epsilon+V_{i,1}^{{\pi}^{*}}(x_{1})-V_{i,1}^{\tilde{\pi}^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})\,,

(A.79)

where
\tilde{\pi}^{\text{PERM}}\in\tilde{\Pi}
such that
|V_{i,1}^{\tilde{\pi}^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})-V_{i,1}^{\pi^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})|\leq H\epsilon
.

By the definition of the oracle in Definition.
4.2
, the algorithm design of Algo.
3
(e.g., we call oracle
\mathbb{O}(\mathcal{D}_{h},\hat{V}_{h+1},\delta/(3nH\mathcal{N}_{(Hn)^{-1}}^{\Pi}))
), and use a union bound over
H
steps,
n
contexts, and
\mathcal{N}_{(Hn)^{-1}}^{\Pi}
policies, we have: with probability at least
1-\delta/3
, the condition in Eq.(
A.70
) holds (with the policy class
\Pi
replaced by
\tilde{\Pi}
(and
\epsilon=1/(Hn))
.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(V_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-V_{i,1}^{\tilde{\pi}^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(V_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-\hat{V}_{i,1}^{\tilde{\pi}^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(V_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-\hat{V}_{i,1}^{\pi^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})\big)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\hat{V}_{i,1}^{\pi^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})-\hat{V}_{i,1}^{\tilde{\pi}^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(V_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-\hat{V}_{i,1}^{\pi^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})\big)+H\cdot\frac{1}{Hn}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(V_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-\hat{V}_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})\big)+1/n\,,

(A.80)

where the first inequality holds because of the pessimism in Lemma
A.72
