, the second inequality holds because
|\hat{V}_{i,1}^{\tilde{\pi}^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})-\hat{V}_{i,1}^{\pi^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})|\leq H\epsilon
with
\epsilon
here specified as
1/(Hn)
, and the last inequality holds because that in the algorithm design of Algo.
4
we set
\pi^{\text{PERM}}=\argmax_{\pi\in\Pi}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\hat{V}^{\pi}_{i,1}(x_{1})
.

Then what left is to bound
V_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-\hat{V}_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})
.

And using Lemma A.1 in
[
110
]
, we have

\displaystyle V_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-\hat{V}_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\pi}^{*},\mathcal{M}_{i}}\big[\iota_{i,h}^{\pi^{*}}(s_{h},a_{h})\,\big|\,s_{1}=x\big]+\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{*},\mathcal{M}_{i}}\big[\iota_{i,h}^{\pi^{*}}(s_{h},a_{h})\,\big|\,s_{1}=x\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt+\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{*},\mathcal{M}_{i}}\big[\langle\hat{Q}^{\pi^{*}}_{i,h}(s_{h},\cdot),\pi^{*}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})-\pi^{*}_{h}(\cdot{\,|\,}s_{h})\rangle_{\mathcal{A}}\,\big|\,s_{1}=x\big]

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{*},\mathcal{M}_{i}}\big[\Gamma_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})\,\big|\,s_{1}=x\big]\,,

(A.81)

where in the last inequality we use Lemma
A.2.2
.

Finally, with Eq.(
A.209
), Eq.(
A.78
), Eq.(
A.79
), Eq.(
A.80
), and Eq.(
A.81
), with
\epsilon
set as
\frac{1}{nH}
, we can get w.p. at least
1-\delta

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}{V_{c,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-V_{c,1}^{\pi^{\text{PERM}}}(x_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{5}{n}+2\sqrt{\frac{2\log(6\mathcal{N}_{(Hn)^{-1}}^{\Pi}/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}{\pi^{*},\mathcal{M}_{i}}{\Gamma_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})|s_{1}=x_{1}}

\displaystyle\leq 7\sqrt{\frac{2\log(6\mathcal{N}_{(Hn)^{-1}}^{\Pi}/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}{\pi^{*},\mathcal{M}_{i}}{\Gamma_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})|s_{1}=x_{1}}\,.

∎

A.2.2.2
Proof of Theorem
4.4.2

Our proof has two steps. First, we define that

\displaystyle\iota_{i,h}(x,a):=\mathbb{B}_{i,h}V_{i,h+1}(x,a)-Q_{i,h}(x,a)

(A.82)

Then we have the following lemma from
[
110
]
:

Lemma A.2.4
.

Define the event
\mathcal{E}
as

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}=\bigg\{\big|(\hat{\mathbb{B}}\hat{V}^{\pi_{i}}_{i,h+1})(x,a)-(\mathbb{B}_{i,h}\hat{V}^{\pi_{i}}_{i,h+1})(x,a)\big|\leq\Gamma_{i,h}(x,a)~\forall(x,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A},\forall h\in[H],\forall i\in[n]\bigg\},

Then by selecting the input parameter
\xi=\delta/(Hn)
in
\mathbb{O}
, we have
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-\delta
and

\displaystyle 0\leq\iota_{i,h}(x,a)\leq 2\Gamma_{i,h}(x,a).

Proof.

The proof is the same as [Lemma 5.1,
[
110
]
] with the probability assigned as
\delta/(Hn)
and a union bound over
h\in[H],i\in[n]
.
∎

Next lemma shows the difference between the value of the optimal policy
\pi^{*}
and number
n
of different policies
\pi_{i}
for
n
MDPs.

Lemma A.2.5
.

Let
\pi
be an arbitrary policy. Then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}[V_{i,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})-V_{i,1}^{\pi^{i}}(x_{1})]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{i,\pi}[\langle Q_{i,h}(\cdot,\cdot),\pi_{h}(\cdot|\cdot)-\pi_{i,h}(\cdot|\cdot)\rangle_{\mathcal{A}}]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{h=1}^{H}(\mathbb{E}_{i,\pi}[\iota_{i,h}(x_{h},a_{h})]-\mathbb{E}_{i,\pi_{i}}[\iota_{i,h}(x_{h},a_{h})])

(A.83)

Proof.

The proof is the same as Lemma 3.1 in
[
110
]
except substituting
\pi
into the lemma.
∎

We also have the following one-step lemma:

Lemma A.2.6

(Lemma 3.3,
[
31
]
)
.

For any distribution
p^{*},p\in\Delta(\mathcal{A})
, if
p^{\prime}(\cdot)\propto p(\cdot)\cdot\exp(\alpha\cdot Q(x,\cdot))
, then

\displaystyle\langle Q(x,\cdot),p^{*}(\cdot)-p(\cdot)\rangle\leq\alpha H^{2}/2+\alpha^{-1}\cdot\bigg(\text{KL}(p^{*}(\cdot)\|p(\cdot))-\text{KL}(p^{*}(\cdot)\|p^{\prime}(\cdot))\bigg).

Given the above lemmas, we begin our proof of Theorem
4.4.2
.

Proof of Theorem
4.4.2
.

Combining Lemma
A.2.4
and Lemma
A.2.5
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}[V_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-V_{i,1}^{\pi^{i}}(x_{1})]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{i,\pi^{*}}[\langle Q_{i,h},\pi_{h}^{*}-\pi_{i,h}\rangle]+2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{i,\pi^{*}}[\Gamma_{i,h}(x_{h},a_{h})]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\alpha H^{2}/2+\alpha^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{i,\pi^{*}}[\text{KL}(\pi_{h}^{*}(\cdot|x_{h})\|\pi_{i,h}(\cdot|x_{h}))-\text{KL}(\pi_{h}^{*}(\cdot|x_{h})\|\pi_{i+1,h}(\cdot|x_{h}))]

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt+2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{i,\pi^{*}}[\Gamma_{i,h}(x_{h},a_{h})]

\displaystyle\leq\alpha H^{3}n/2+\alpha^{-1}\cdot\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{i,\pi^{*}}[\text{KL}(\pi_{h}^{*}(\cdot|x_{h})\|\pi_{1,h}(\cdot|x_{h}))]+2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{i,\pi^{*}}[\Gamma_{i,h}(x_{h},a_{h})]

\displaystyle\leq\alpha H^{3}n/2+\alpha^{-1}H\log|A|+2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{i,\pi^{*}}[\Gamma_{i,h}(x_{h},a_{h})],

where the last inequality holds since
\pi_{1,h}
is the uniform distribution over
\mathcal{A}
. Then, selecting
\alpha=1/\sqrt{H^{2}n}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}[V_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-V_{i,1}^{\pi^{i}}(x_{1})]\leq 2\sqrt{n\log|A|H^{2}}+2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{i,\pi^{*}}[\Gamma_{i,h}(s_{h},a_{h})],

which holds for the random selection of
\mathcal{D}
with probability at least
1-\delta
. Meanwhile, note that each MDP
M_{i}
is drawn i.i.d. from
C
. Meanwhile, note that
\pi_{i}
only depends on MDP
M_{1},...,M_{i-1}
. Therefore, by the standard online-to-batch conversion, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[V_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-V_{i,1}^{\pi_{i}}(x_{1})]+\bigg(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}V_{c,1}^{\pi_{i}}(x_{1})-\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}V_{c,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})\bigg)\leq 2H\sqrt{\frac{2\log 1/\delta}{n}}\bigg)\geq 1-\delta,

which suggests that with probability at least
1-2\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}V_{c,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}V_{c,1}^{\pi_{i}}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{\log|A|H^{2}}{n}}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{\pi^{*}}[\Gamma_{i,h}(x_{h},a_{h})]+2\sqrt{\frac{2H\log 1/\delta}{n}}.

Therefore, by selecting
\pi^{\text{PPPO}}:=\text{random}(\pi_{1},...,\pi_{n})
and applying the Markov inequality, setting
\delta=1/8
, we have our bound holds.
∎

A.2.3
Suboptimality bounds for real-world setups

In this section we state and prove the suboptimality bounds we promised in Remarks
9
and
11
, where we merge the sampled contexts into
m
groups (generally,
m<<n
) to reduce the computational complexity in practical settings.

Assume
m|n
and the
n
contexts from offline dataset are equally partitioned into
m
groups. We write the resulting average MDPs (see Proposition
4.1
) for each group as
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{1},\ldots,\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{m}
. For each
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{j}
, we regard it as an individual context in the sense of (
A.70
) and denote the resulting uncertainty quantifier and value function as
{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{j,h},{V^{\prime}}^{\pi}_{j,h}
.

Theorem A.2.7

(Suboptimality bound for Remark
9
)
.

Assume the same setting as Theorem
4.4.1
with the original
n
contexts grouped as
m
contexts, and denote the resulting algorithm as PERM-
m
V. Then w.p. at least
1-\delta
, the output
\pi^{\prime}
of PERM-
m
V satisfies

\displaystyle\text{SubOpt}(\pi^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{2\sqrt{\frac{2\log(6\mathcal{N}_{(Hm)^{-1}}^{\Pi}/\delta)}{n}}}_{I_{1}:\text{Supervised learning (SL) error}}+\underbrace{\frac{2}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}{\pi^{*},\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{j}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{j,h}(s_{h},a_{h})|s_{1}=x_{1}}}_{I_{2}:\text{Reinforcement learning (RL) error}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\frac{5}{m}+2\sup_{\pi}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{\pi}_{i,1}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{V^{\prime}}^{\pi}_{j,1}(x_{1})\right|}_{\text{Additional approximation error}},

where
\mathbb{E}_{j,\pi^{*}}
is w.r.t. the trajectory induced by
\pi^{*}
with the transition
\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{j}
in the underlying average MDP
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{j}
.

Proof of Theorem
A.2.7
.

Similar to the proof of Theorem
4.4.1
, we decompose the suboptimality gap as follows

\displaystyle\text{SubOpt}(\pi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}{V_{c,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-V_{c,1}^{\pi^{\prime}}(x_{1})}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}{V_{c,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{\pi^{*}}_{i,1}(x_{1})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i,1}^{\pi^{\prime}}(x_{1})-\mathbb{E}_{c\sim C}{V_{c,1}^{\pi^{\prime}}(x_{1})}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{\pi^{*}}_{i,1}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{V^{\prime}}^{\pi^{*}}_{j,1}(x_{1})+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{V^{\prime}}_{j,1}^{\pi^{\prime}}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i,1}^{\pi^{\prime}}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\big({V^{\prime}}_{j,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-{V^{\prime}}_{j,1}^{\pi^{\prime}}(x_{1})\big)\,.

(A.84)

Note that we can bound the first and third lines of (
A.84
) with the exactly same arguments as the proof of Theorem
4.4.1
, the only notation-wise difference is that the uncertainty quantifier becomes
\Gamma^{\prime}
as we are operating on the level of average MDP
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{j}
.

The only thing left is to bound the second line of (
A.84
). This is the same in spirit of the bound (
A.67
), so that we can express the bound as follows

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{\pi^{*}}_{i,1}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{V^{\prime}}^{\pi^{*}}_{j,1}(x_{1})+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{V^{\prime}}_{j,1}^{\pi^{\prime}}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i,1}^{\pi^{\prime}}(x_{1})

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{\pi}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{\pi}_{i,1}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{V^{\prime}}^{\pi}_{j,1}(x_{1})\right|.

To conclude, our final bound can be expressed as: with
\epsilon
set as
\frac{1}{mH}
, we can get w.p. at least
1-\delta

\displaystyle\text{SubOpt}(\pi^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{2\log(6\mathcal{N}_{(Hm)^{-1}}^{\Pi}/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{2}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}{\pi^{*},\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{j}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{j,h}(s_{h},a_{h})|s_{1}=x_{1}}

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt+\frac{5}{m}+2\sup_{\pi}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V^{\pi}_{i,1}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{V^{\prime}}^{\pi}_{j,1}(x_{1})\right|.

∎

To prove the suboptimality bound for Remark
11
, we denote that the policies produced by PPPO after merging dataset to
m
groups to be
\pi_{1},\ldots,\pi_{m}
, and the original PPPO algorithm would produce the policies as
\pi^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\pi^{\prime}_{n}
. We assume that the merging of dataset from
n
to
m
groups is only to combine the consecutive
n/m
terms from
\pi^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\pi^{\prime}_{n}
and preserves the order.

Theorem A.2.8

(Suboptimality bound for Remark
11
)
.

Assume the same setting as Theorem
4.4.2
with the original
n
contexts grouped as
m
contexts, and denote the resulting algorithm as PPPO-
m
V. Let
{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{j,h}
be the uncertainty quantifier returned by
\mathbb{O}
through the PPPO-
m
V algorithm. Selecting
\alpha=1/\sqrt{H^{2}m}
. Then selecting
\delta=1/8
, w.p. at least
2/3
, we have

\displaystyle\text{SubOpt}(\pi^{\text{PPPO}-mV})

\displaystyle\leq 10\bigg(\underbrace{\sqrt{\frac{\log|\mathcal{A}|H^{2}}{m}}}_{I_{1}:\text{SL error}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}{j,\pi^{*}}{{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{j,h}(s_{h},a_{h})|s_{1}=x_{1}}}_{I_{2}:\text{RL error}}

\displaystyle+\sup_{\pi}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{V^{\prime}}_{j,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})\right|+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sup_{\pi}\left|\mathbb{E}_{c}[V_{c,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})]-V_{i,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})\right|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sup_{\pi}\left|\mathbb{E}_{c}[{V^{\prime}}_{c,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})]-{V^{\prime}}_{j,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})\right|\bigg).

where
\mathbb{E}_{j,\pi^{*}}
is w.r.t. the trajectory induced by
\pi^{*}
with the transition
\bar{\mathcal{P}}_{j}
in the underlying MDP
\bar{\mathcal{M}}_{j}
.

Proof of Theorem
A.2.8
.

Using the same arguments as in the proof of Theorem
4.4.2
with
\alpha=1/\sqrt{H^{2}m}
, we can derive the bound

\sum_{j=1}^{m}[{V^{\prime}}_{j,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-{V^{\prime}}_{j,1}^{\pi_{j}}(x_{1})]\leq 2\sqrt{m\log|A|H^{2}}+2\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{j,\pi^{*}}[{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{j,h}(s_{h},a_{h})].

Leveraging this bound and online-to-batch, we obtain the following estimation

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{c}[V^{\pi^{\ast}}_{c,1}(x_{1})]-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{c}[V^{\pi_{j}}_{c,1}(x_{1})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{c}[V^{\pi^{\ast}}_{c,1}(x_{1})]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{c}[V^{\pi^{\prime}_{i}}_{c,1}(x_{1})]+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}_{c}[V^{\pi^{\prime}_{i}}_{c,1}(x_{1})]-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{c}[V^{\pi_{j}}_{c,1}(x_{1})]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2H\sqrt{\frac{2\log 1/\delta}{n}}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{c}[V_{c,1}^{\pi^{\prime}_{i}}(x_{1})]-V_{i,1}^{\pi^{\prime}_{i}}(x_{1})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{c}[V^{\pi_{j}}_{c,1}(x_{1})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2H\sqrt{\frac{2\log 1/\delta}{n}}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{V^{\prime}}_{j,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{V^{\prime}}_{j,1}^{\pi^{*}}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{V^{\prime}}_{j,1}^{\pi_{j}}(x_{1})

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{E}_{c}[V_{c,1}^{\pi^{\prime}_{i}}(x_{1})]-V_{i,1}^{\pi^{\prime}_{i}}(x_{1})\right)+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{V^{\prime}}_{j,1}^{\pi_{j}}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}_{c}[V^{\pi_{j}}_{c,1}(x_{1})]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2H\sqrt{\frac{2\log 1/\delta}{n}}+\sup_{\pi}\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}V_{i,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{V^{\prime}}_{j,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})\right|

\displaystyle+2\sqrt{\frac{\log|A|H^{2}}{m}}+\frac{2}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbb{E}_{j,\pi^{*}}[{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{j,h}(s_{h},a_{h})]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sup_{\pi}\left|\mathbb{E}_{c}[V_{c,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})]-V_{i,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})\right|+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sup_{\pi}\left|\mathbb{E}_{c}[{V^{\prime}}_{c,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})]-{V^{\prime}}_{j,1}^{\pi}(x_{1})\right|.

Finally we apply Markov inequality and take
\delta=1/8
as in the proof of Theorem
4.4.2
.
∎

A.2.4
Results in Section
4.5

A.2.4.1
Proof of Theorem
4.5.1

By
[
110
]
, the parameters specified as
\lambda=1,\hskip 11.74988pt\beta(\delta)=c\cdot dH\sqrt{\log(2dHK/\delta)}
, and applying union bound, we can get:
for Algo.
6
, with probability at least
1-\delta/3

\displaystyle\big|(\hat{\mathbb{B}}_{i,h}\hat{V}^{\pi}_{i,h+1})(x,a)-(\mathbb{B}_{i,h}\hat{V}^{\pi}_{i,h+1})(x,a)\big|\leq\beta\big(\frac{\delta}{3nH\mathcal{N}_{(Hn)^{-1}}^{\Pi}}\big)\bigl(\phi(x,a)^{\top}\Lambda_{i,h}^{-1}\phi(x,a)\bigr)^{1/2}\,,

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt\text{for all}~i\in[n],\pi\in\tilde{\Pi},(x,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A},h\in[H]\,,

(A.85)

where
\tilde{\Pi}
is the
\frac{1}{Hn}
-covering set of the policy space
\Pi
w.r.t. distance
\mathrm{d}(\pi^{1},\pi^{2})=\max_{s\in\mathcal{S},h\in[H]}\|\pi^{1}_{h}(\cdot|s)-\pi^{2}_{h}(\cdot|s)\|_{1}
.

Therefore, we can specify the
\Gamma_{i,h}(\cdot,\cdot)
in Theorem
4.4.1
with
\beta\big(\frac{\delta}{3nH\mathcal{N}_{(Hn)^{-1}}^{\Pi}}\big)\bigl(\phi(x,a)^{\top}\Lambda_{i,h}^{-1}\phi(x,a)\bigr)^{1/2}
, and follow the same process as the proof of Theorem
4.4.1
to get the result for Algo.
4
with subroutine Algo.
6
.

Similarly, we can get: we can get:
for Algo.
6
, with probability at least
1-1/4

\displaystyle\big|(\hat{\mathbb{B}}_{i,h}\hat{V}_{i,h+1})(x,a)-(\mathbb{B}_{i,h}\hat{V}_{i,h+1})(x,a)\big|\leq\beta\big(\frac{\delta}{4nH}\big)\bigl(\phi(x,a)^{\top}\Lambda_{i,h}^{-1}\phi(x,a)\bigr)^{1/2}\,,

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt\text{for all}~i\in[n],(x,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A},h\in[H]\,.

(A.86)

Therefore, we can specify the
\Gamma_{i,h}(\cdot,\cdot)
in Theorem
4.4.2
with
\beta\big(\frac{\delta}{4nH}\big)\bigl(\phi(x,a)^{\top}\Lambda_{i,h}^{-1}\phi(x,a)\bigr)^{1/2}
and follow the same process as the proof of Theorem
4.4.2
to get the result for Algo.
5
with subroutine Algo.
6
.

A.2.4.2
Proof of Corollary
4.1

By the assumption that
\mathcal{D}_{i}
is generated by behavior policy
\bar{\pi}_{i}
which well-explores MDP
\mathcal{M}_{i}
with constant
c_{i}
(where the well-explore is defined in Def.
4.3
), the proof of Corollary 4.6 in
[
110
]
, and applying a union bound over
n
contexts, we have that for Algo.
4
with subroutine Algo.
6
w.p. at least
1-\delta/2

\displaystyle\|\phi(x,a)\|_{\Lambda_{i,h}^{-1}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2d}{c_{i}K}}

\displaystyle~\textrm{for all}~i\in[n],~(x,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\text{ and all }h\in[H]\,,

(A.87)

and for Algo.
4
with subroutine Algo.
6
w.p. at least
1-\delta/2

\displaystyle\|\phi(x,a)\|_{\Lambda_{i,h}^{-1}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2dH}{c_{i}K}}

\displaystyle~\textrm{for all}~i\in[n],~(x,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\text{ and all }h\in[H]\,,

(A.88)

because we use the data splitting technique and we only utilize each trajectory once for one data tuple at some stage
h
, so we replace
K
with
K/H
.

Then, the result follows by plugging the results above into Theorem
4.5.1
.

A.3
Appendix for Chapter
5

A.3.1
More Discussions on Related Work

In this section, we will give more comparisons and discussions on some previous works that are related to our work to some extent.

There are some other works on bandits leveraging user (or task) relations, which have some relations with the clustering of bandits (CB) works to some extent, but are in different lines of research from CB, and are quite different from our work. First, besides CB, the work
[
246
]
also leverages user relations. Specifically, it utilizes a
known
user adjacency graph to share context and payoffs among neighbors, whereas in CB, the user relations are
unknown
and need to be learnt, thus the setting differs a lot from CB. Second, there are lines of works on multi-task learning
[
34
,
57
,
206
,
36
,
231
,
230
]
, meta-learning
[
223
,
94
,
35
]
and federated learning
[
205
,
98
]
, where multiple different tasks are solved jointly and share information. Note that all of these works do not assume an underlying
unknown
user clustering structure which needs to be inferred by the agent to speed up learning. For works on multi-task learning
[
34
,
57
,
206
,
36
,
231
,
230
]
, they assume the tasks are related but no user clustering structures, and to the best of our knowledge, none of them consider model misspefications, thus differing a lot from ours. For some recent works on meta-learning
[
223
,
94
,
222
]
, they propose general Bayesian hierarchical models to share knowledge across tasks, and design Thompson-Sampling-based algorithms to optimize the Bayes regret, which are quite different from the line of CB works, and differ a lot from ours.
And additionally, as supported by the discussions in the works
[
35
,
230
]
, multi-task learning and meta-learning are different lines of research from CB. For the works on federated learning
[
205
,
98
]
, they consider the privacy and communication costs among multiple servers, whose setting is also very different from the previous CB works and our work.

Remark.
Again, we emphasize that the goal of this work is to initialize the study of the important CBMUM problem, and propose general design ideas for dealing with model misspecifications in CB problems. Therefore, our study is based on fundamental models on CB
[
81
,
142
]
and MLB
[
130
]
, and the algorithm design ideas and theoretical analysis are pretty general. We leave incorporating the more recent model selection methods
[
179
,
70
]
into our framework to address the unknown exact maximum model misspecification level as an interesting future work. It would also be interesting to consider incorporating our methods and ideas of tackling model misspecifications into the studies of multi-task learning, meta learning and federated learning.

A.3.2
More Discussions on Assumptions

All the assumptions (Assumptions
9.2
,
9.3
,
9.4
,
9.1
)in this work are natural and basically follow (or less strigent than) previous works on CB and MLB
[
81
,
139
,
142
,
153
,
130
]
.

A.3.2.1
Less Strigent Assumption on on the Generating Distribution of Arm Vectors

We also make some contributions to relax a widely-used but stringent assumption on the generating distribution of arm vectors. Specifically, our Assumption
9.4
on item regularity relaxes the previous one used in previous CB works
[
81
,
139
,
142
,
153
]
by removing the condition that the variance should be upper bounded by
\frac{\lambda^{2}}{8\log(4\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|)}
. For technical details on this, please refer to the theoretical analysis and discussions in Appendix
A.3.10
.

A.3.2.2
Discussions on Assumption
9.1
about Bounded Misspecification Level

This assumption follows
[
130
]
. Note that this
\epsilon_{*}
can be an upper bound on the maximum misspecification
level, not the exact maximum itself. In real-world applications, the deviations are usually small
[
84
]
, and we can set a relatively big
\epsilon_{*}
(e.g., 0.2) to be the upper bound. Our experimental results support this claim. As shown in our experimental results on real-data case 2, even when
\epsilon_{*}
is unknown, our algorithms still perform well by setting
\epsilon_{*}=0.2
.
Some recent studies
[
179
,
70
]
use model selection methods to theoretically
deal with unknown exact maximum misspecification level in the single-user case, which is not the emphasis of this work. Additionally, the work
[
70
]
assumes that the learning agent has access to a regression oracle. And for the work
[
179
]
, though their regret bound is dependent on the exact maximum misspecification level that needs not to be known by the agent, an upper bound of the exact maximum misspecification level is still needed.
We leave incorporating their methods to deal with unknown exact maximum misspecification level as an interesting future work.

A.3.2.3
Discussions on Assumption
9.3
about the Theoretical Results under General User Arrival Distributions

The uniform arrival in Assumption
9.3
follows previous CB works
[
81
,
139
,
153
]
, it only affects the
T_{0}
term, which is the time after which the algorithm maintains a “good partition” and is of
O(u\log T)
. For an arbitrary arrival distribution,
T_{0}
becomes
O(1/p_{min}\log T)
, where
p_{min}
is the minimal arrival probability of a user. And since it is a lower-order term (of
O(\log T)
), it will not affect the main order of our regret upper bound which is of
O(\epsilon_{*}T\sqrt{md\log T}+d\sqrt{mT}\log T)
. The work
[
142
]
studies arbitrary arrivals and aims to remove the
1/p_{min}
factor in this term, but their setting is different. They make an additional assumption that users in the same cluster not only have the same preference vector, but also the same arrival probability, which is different from our setting and other classic CB works
[
81
,
139
,
153
]
where we only assume users in the same cluster share the same preference vector.

A.3.3
Highlight of the Theoretical Analysis

Our proof flow and methodologies are novel in clustering of bandits (CB), which are expected to inspire future works on model misspecifications and CB. The main challenge of the regret analysis in CBMUM is that due to the estimation inaccuracy caused by misspecifications, it is impossible to cluster all users exactly correctly, and it is highly non-trivial to
bound the regret caused by
“misclustering"
\zeta
-close users
.

To the best of our knowledge, the common proof flow of previous CB works (e.g.,
[
81
,
139
,
153
]
) can be summarized in two steps: The first is to prove a sufficient time
T^{\prime}_{0}
after which the algorithms can cluster all users
exactly correctly
with high probability. Note that the inferred clustering structure remains static after
T^{\prime}_{0}
, making the analysis easy. Second, after the
correct static clustering
, the regret can be trivially bounded by bounding
m
(number of underlying clusters) independent linear bandit algorithms, resulting in a
O(d\sqrt{mT}\log T)
regret.

The above common proof flow is straightforward in CB with perfectly linear models, but it would fail to get a non-vacuous regret bound for CBMUM.
In CBMUM, it is impossible to learn an exactly correct static clustering structure with model misspecifications. In particular, we prove that we can only expect the algorithm to cluster
\zeta
-close users together rather than cluster all users exactly correctly. Therefore, the previous flow can not be applied to the more challenging CBMUM problem.

We do the following to address the challenges in obtaining a tight regret bound for CBMUM. With the carefully-designed novel key components of RCLUMB, we can prove a sufficient time
T_{0}
after which RCLUMB can get a “good partition" (Definition
5.4
) with high probability, which means the cluster
\overline{V_{t}}
assigned to
i_{t}
contains all users in the same ground-truth cluster as
i_{t}
, and possibly some other
i_{t}
’s
\zeta
-close users. Intuitively, after
T_{0}
, the algorithm can leverage all the information from the users’ ground-truth clusters but may misuse some information from other
\zeta
-close users with preference gaps up to
\zeta
, causing a regret of
“misclustering"
\zeta
-close users
. It is highly non-trivial to bound this part of regret, and the proof methods would be beneficial for future studies in CB in challenging cases when it is impossible to cluster all users exactly correctly. For details, please refer to the discussions “(ii) Bounding the term of misclustering it’s
\zeta
-close users" in Section
5.4
, the key Lemma
5.4.4
(Bound of error caused by misclustering), its proof and tightness discussion in Appendix
A.3.7
. Also, a more subtle analysis is needed to handle
the time-varying inferred clustering structure since the “good partition" may change over time, whereas in the previous CB works, the clustering structure remains static after
T_{0}^{\prime}
. For theoretical details on this, please refer to
Appendix
A.3.5
.

A.3.4
Discussions on why Trivially Combining Existing CB and MLB Works Could Not Achieve a Non-vacuous Regret Upper Bound

We consider discussing regret upper bounds for CB without considering misspecifications for three cases: (1) neither the clustering process nor the decision process considers misspecifications (previous CB algorithms); (2) the decision process does not consider misspecifications; (3) the clustering process does not consider misspecifications.

For cases (1) and (2), the decision process could contribute to the leading regret. We consider the case where there are
m
underlying clusters, with each cluster’s arrival being
T/m
, and the agent knows the underlying clustering structure. For this case, there exist some instances where the regret upper bound
R(T)
is strictly larger than
\epsilon_{*}T\sqrt{m\log T}
asymptotically in
T
. Formally, in the discussion of “Failure of unmodified algorithm" in Appendix E in
[
130
]
,
they give an example to show that in the single-user case, the regret
R_{1}(T)
of the classic linear bandit algorithms without considering misspecifications will have:
\displaystyle\lim_{T\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{R_{1}(T)}{\epsilon_{*}T\sqrt{m\log T}}=+\infty
. In our problem with multiple users and
m
underlying clusters, even if we know the underlying clustering structure and keep
m
independent linear bandit algorithms with
T_{i}
for the cluster
i\in[m]
to leverage the common information of clusters, the best we can get is
R_{2}(T)=\sum_{i\in[m]}R_{1}(T_{i})
. By the above results, if the decision process does not consider misspecifications, we have
\displaystyle\lim_{T\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{R_{2}(T)}{\epsilon_{*}T\sqrt{m\log T}}=\displaystyle\lim_{T\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{mR_{1}(T/m)}{\epsilon_{*}T\sqrt{m\log T}}=+\infty
. Recall that the regret upper bound
R(T)
of our proposed algorithms is of
O(\epsilon_{*}T\sqrt{md\log T}+d\sqrt{mT}\log T)
(thus, we have
\displaystyle\lim_{T\rightarrow+\infty}\frac{R(T)}{\epsilon_{*}T\sqrt{m\log T}}<+\infty
), which gives a proof that that the regret upper bound of our proposed algorithms is asymptotically much better than CB algorithms in cases (1)(2).

For case (3), if the clustering process does not use the more tolerant deletion rule in Line
10

of Algo.
7
, the gap between users linked by edges would possibly exceed
\zeta
(
\zeta=2\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}}
) even after
T_{0}
, which will result in a regret upper bound no better than
O(\epsilon_{*}u\sqrt{d}T)
. As the number of users
u
is usually huge in practice, this result is vacuous. The reasons for getting the above claim are as follows. Even if the clustering process further uses our deletion rule considering misspecifications, and the users linked by edges are within
\zeta
distance, failing to extract
1
-hop users (Line
5
in Algo.
7
) would cause the leading
O(\epsilon_{*}u\sqrt{d}T)
regret term, as in the worst case, the preference vector
\theta
of the user in
\tilde{V}_{t}
who is
h
-hop away from user
i_{t}
could deviate by
h\zeta
from
\theta_{i_{t}}
, where
h
can be as large as
u
, and it would make the second term in Eq.(
5.8
) a
O(\epsilon_{*}u\sqrt{d}T)
term. If we completely do not consider the misspecifications in the clustering process, the above user gap between users linked by edges would possibly exceed
\zeta
, which will cause a regret upper bound worse than
O(\epsilon_{*}u\sqrt{d}T)
.

A.3.5
Proof of Theorem
6.4.3

We first prove the result in the case when
\gamma_{1}
defined in Definition
5.2
is not infinity, i.e.,
4\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}}<\gamma_{1}<\infty
. The proof of the special case when
\gamma_{1}=\infty
will directly follow the proof of this case.

For the instantaneous regret
R_{t}
at round
t
, with probability at least
1-5\delta
for some
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{5})
, at
\forall{t\geq T_{0}}
:

\displaystyle R_{t}

\displaystyle=(\bm{x}_{a_{t}^{*}}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}+\bm{\epsilon}^{i_{t},t}_{a_{t}^{*}})-(\bm{x}_{a_{t}}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}+\bm{\epsilon}^{i_{t},t}_{a_{t}})

(A.89)

\displaystyle=\bm{x}_{a_{t}^{*}}^{\top}(\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1})+(\bm{x}_{a_{t}^{*}}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}+C_{a_{t}^{*},t})-(\bm{x}_{a_{t}}^{\top}\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}+C_{a_{t},t})

\displaystyle+\bm{x}_{a_{t}}^{\top}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}})+C_{a_{t},t}-C_{a_{t}^{*},t}+(\bm{\epsilon}^{i_{t},t}_{a_{t}^{*}}-\bm{\epsilon}^{i_{t},t}_{a_{t}})

\displaystyle\leq 2C_{a_{t},t}+\frac{2\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{2d}}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mathbb{I}\{\overline{V}_{t}\notin\mathcal{V}\}+2\epsilon_{*}\,,

where the last inequality holds by the UCB arm selection strategy in Eq.(
6.3
), the concentration bound given in Lemma
6.4.2
, and the fact that
\left\lVert\bm{\epsilon}^{i,t}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\epsilon_{*},\forall{i\in\mathcal{U}},\forall{t}
.

We define the following events. Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{0}

\displaystyle=\{R_{t}\leq 2C_{a_{t},t}+\frac{2\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{2d}}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mathbb{I}\{\overline{V}_{t}\notin\mathcal{V}\}+2\epsilon_{*},

\displaystyle\text{for all }\{t:t\geq T_{0},\text{and the algorithm maintains a ``good partition" at $t$}\}\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}

\displaystyle=\{\text{the algorithm maintains a ``good partition" for all }t\geq T_{0}\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{E}_{0}\cap\mathcal{E}_{1}\,.

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{0})\geq 1-2\delta
. According to Lemma
A.3.2
,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E}_{1})\geq 1-3\delta
. Thus,
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\geq 1-5\delta
for some
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{5})
. Take
\delta=\frac{1}{T}
, we can get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[R(T)]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{E})\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}R(T)+\mathbb{P}(\overline{\mathcal{E}})\mathbb{I}\{\overline{\mathcal{E}}\}R(T)

(A.90)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}R(T)+5\times\frac{1}{T}\times T

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}R(T)+5\,,

where
\overline{\mathcal{E}}
denotes the complementary event of
\mathcal{E}
,
\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}R(T)
denotes
R(T)
under event
\mathcal{E}
,
\mathbb{I}\{\overline{\mathcal{E}}\}R(T)
denotes
R(T)
under event
\overline{\mathcal{E}}
, and we use
R(T)\leq T
to bound
R(T)
under event
\overline{\mathcal{E}}
.

Then it remains to bound
\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}R(T)
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}R(T)

\displaystyle\leq R(T_{0})+\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}R_{t}]

\displaystyle\leq T_{0}+2\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}C_{a_{t},t}]+\frac{2\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{2d}}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E},\overline{V}_{t}\notin\mathcal{V}\}]+2\epsilon_{*}T

(A.91)

\displaystyle=T_{0}+2\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}C_{a_{t},t}]+\frac{2\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{2d}}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E},\overline{V}_{t}\notin\mathcal{V}\})+2\epsilon_{*}T

\displaystyle\leq T_{0}+2\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}C_{a_{t},t}]+\frac{2\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{2d}}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\times\frac{\tilde{u}}{u}T+2\epsilon_{*}T\,,

(A.92)

where Eq.(
A.91
) follows from Eq.(
A.195
). Eq.(
A.92
) holds since under Assumption
9.3
about user arrival uniformness and by Definition
5.4
of “good partition",
\mathbb{P}(\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E},\overline{V}_{t}\notin\mathcal{V}\})\leq\frac{\tilde{u}}{u},\forall{t\geq T_{0}}
, where
\tilde{u}
is defined in Definition
5.3
.

Then we need to bound
\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}C_{a_{t},t}]
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}C_{a_{t},t}

\displaystyle=\Big(\sqrt{\lambda}+\sqrt{2\log(\frac{1}{\delta})+d\log(1+\frac{T}{\lambda d})}\Big)\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\epsilon_{*}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\left|\bm{x}_{a_{t}}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\right|\,.

(A.93)

Next, we bound the
\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}}
term in Eq.(
A.93
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\sum_{k=1}^{m_{t}}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in\tilde{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}\}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k},t-1}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in V_{j}\}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},t-1}^{-1}}

(A.94)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sqrt{\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in V_{j}\}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in V_{j}\}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},t-1}^{-1}}^{2}}

(A.95)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sqrt{2T_{V_{j},T}d\log(1+\frac{T}{\lambda d})}

(A.96)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\sqrt{2\sum_{j=1}^{m}1\sum_{j=1}^{m}T_{V_{j},T}d\log(1+\frac{T}{\lambda d})}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\sqrt{2mdT\log(1+\frac{T}{\lambda d})}\,,

(A.97)

where we use
m_{t}
to denote the number of connected components partitioned by the algorithm at
t
,
\tilde{V}^{\prime}_{t,k},k\in[m_{t}]
to denote the connected components partitioned by the algorithm at
t
,
\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}\subseteq\tilde{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}
to denote the subset extracted to be the cluster
\overline{V}_{t}
for
i_{t}
from
\tilde{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}
conditioned on
i_{t}\in\tilde{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}
, and
T_{V_{j},T}
to denote the number of times that the served users lie in the
ground-truth cluster

V_{j}
up to time
T
, i.e.,
T_{V_{j},T}=\sum_{t\in[T]}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in V_{j}\}
.

The reasons for having Eq.(
A.94
) are as follows. Under event
\mathcal{E}
, the algorithm will always have a “good partition" after
T_{0}
. By Definition
5.4
and the proof process of Lemma
A.3.2
about the edge deletion conditions, we can get
m_{t}\leq m
and if
i_{t}\in\tilde{V}^{\prime}_{t,k},i_{t}\in V_{j}
, then
V_{j}\subseteq\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}
since
\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}
contains
V_{j}
and possibly other
ground-truth clusters

V_{n},n\in[m]
, whose preference vectors are
\zeta
-close to
\bm{\theta}^{j}
. Therefore, by the definition of the regularized Gramian matrix, we can get
M_{\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k},t-1}\succeq M_{V_{j},t-1},\forall{t\geq T_{0}+1}
. Thus by the above reasoning,
\sum_{k=1}^{m_{t}}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in\tilde{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}\}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k},t-1}^{-1}}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in V_{j}\}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},t-1}^{-1}},\forall{t\geq T_{0}+1}
.
Eq.(
A.223
) holds by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality; Eq.(
A.96
) follows by the following technical Lemma
A.4.2
. Eq.(
A.97
) is from the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and the fact that
\sum_{j=1}^{m}T_{V_{j},T}=T
.

We then bound the last term in Eq.(
A.93
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\epsilon_{*}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\left|\bm{x}_{a_{t}}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\right|

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\epsilon_{*}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\sum_{k=1}^{m_{t}}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in\tilde{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}\}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}}\left|\bm{x}_{a_{t}}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k},t-1}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\epsilon_{*}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\sum_{k=1}^{m_{t}}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in\tilde{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}\}\sqrt{\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}}1\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}}\left|\bm{x}_{a_{t}}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k},t-1}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\right|^{2}}

(A.98)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\epsilon_{*}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\sum_{k=1}^{m_{t}}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in\tilde{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}\}\sqrt{T_{\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k},t-1}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k},t-1}^{-1}}^{2}}

(A.99)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\epsilon_{*}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{m_{t}}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in\tilde{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}\}\sum_{k=1}^{m_{t}}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in\tilde{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}\}T_{\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k},t-1}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k},t-1}^{-1}}^{2}}

(A.100)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{T}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{m_{t}}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in\tilde{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}\}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k},t-1}^{-1}}^{2}}

(A.101)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{T}\sqrt{\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}1\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\sum_{k=1}^{m_{t}}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in\tilde{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}\}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k},t-1}^{-1}}^{2}}

(A.102)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{T}\sqrt{T\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in V_{j}\}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},t-1}^{-1}}^{2}}

(A.103)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\epsilon_{*}T\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in V_{j}\}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},t-1}^{-1}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{I}\{\mathcal{E}\}\epsilon_{*}T\sqrt{2md\log(1+\frac{T}{\lambda d})}\,,

(A.104)

where Eq.(
A.98
), Eq.(
A.100
) and Eq.(
A.102
) hold because of the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, Eq.(
A.99
) holds since
\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k},t-1}\succeq\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}
, Eq.(
A.101
) is because
T_{\overline{V}^{\prime}_{t,k},t-1}\leq T
, Eq. (
A.103
) follows from the same reasoning as Eq.(
A.94
), and Eq.(
A.104
) comes from the following technical Lemma
A.4.2
.

Finally, plugging Eq.(
A.97
) and Eq.(
A.104
) into Eq.(
A.93
), take expectation and plug it into Eq.(
A.92
), we can get:

\displaystyle R(T)\leq

\displaystyle 5+T_{0}+\frac{\tilde{u}}{u}\times\frac{2\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{2d}T}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}+2\epsilon_{*}T\bigg(1+\sqrt{2md\log(1+\frac{T}{\lambda d})}\bigg)

\displaystyle+2\bigg(\sqrt{\lambda}+\sqrt{2\log(T)+d\log(1+\frac{T}{\lambda d})}\bigg)\times\sqrt{2mdT\log(1+\frac{T}{\lambda d})}\,,

(A.105)

where

T_{0}=16u\log(\frac{u}{\delta})+4u\max\max\{\frac{8d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}-\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}})^{2}}\log(\frac{u}{\delta}),\frac{16}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\log(\frac{8d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}\delta})\}

is given in the following Lemma
A.3.2
in Appendix
A.3.8
.

A.3.6
Proof and Discussions of Theorem
6.4.4

In the work
[
130
]
, they give a lower bound for misspecified linear bandits with a single user. The lower bound of
R(T)
is given by:

R_{3}(T)\geq\epsilon_{*}T\sqrt{d}
. Therefore, suppose our problem with multiple users and
m
underlying clusters where the arrival times are
T_{i}
for each cluster, then for any algorithms, even if they know the underlying clustering structure and keep
m
independent linear bandit algorithms to leverage the common information of clusters, the best they can get is
R(T)=\sum_{i\in[m]}R_{3}(T_{i})\geq\epsilon_{*}\sum_{i\in[m]}T_{i}\sqrt{d}=\epsilon_{*}T\sqrt{d}
, which gives a lower bound of
O(\epsilon_{*}T\sqrt{d})
for the CBMUM problem. Recall that the regret upper bound of our algorithms is of
O(\epsilon_{*}T\sqrt{md\log T}+d\sqrt{mT}\log T)
, asymptotically matching this lower bound with respect to
T
up to logarithmic factors and with respect to
m
up to
O(\sqrt{m})
factors, showing the tightness of our theoretical results (where

m
are typically very small for real applications).

We conjecture that the gap for the
m
factor is due to the strong assumption that cluster structures are known to prove our lower bound, and whether there exists a tighter lower bound will be left for future work.

A.3.7
Proof of the key Lemma
5.4.4

In Lemma
5.4.4
, we want to bound
\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}(\bm{\theta}_{i_{s}}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}})\right|
. By the definition of “good partition", we have
\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i_{s}}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\zeta\,,\forall{i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}
. It is an easy-to-be-made mistake to directly drag
\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i_{s}}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}\right\rVert_{2}
out to upper bound it by
\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}\right\rVert_{2}\times\zeta
and then proceed. We need more careful analysis.

We first prove the following general lemma.

Lemma A.3.1
.

For vectors
\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2},\ldots,\bm{x}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\left\lVert\bm{x}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}\leq 1,\forall{i\in[k]}
, and vectors
\bm{\theta}_{1},\bm{\theta}_{2},\ldots,\bm{\theta}_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}\leq C,\forall{i\in[k]}
, where
C>0
is a constant, we have:

\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}\leq C\sqrt{d}\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\right\rVert_{2}\,.

Proof.

Let
\bm{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k}
be a matrix such that it has
\bm{x}_{i}
s as its columns, i.e.,
\bm{X}=[\bm{x}_{1},\ldots,\bm{x}_{k}]=\begin{bmatrix}\bm{x}_{11}&x_{21}&\cdots&\bm{x}_{k1}\\
\bm{x}_{12}&x_{22}&\cdots&\bm{x}_{k2}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\bm{x}_{1d}&x_{2d}&\cdots&\bm{x}_{kd}\end{bmatrix}.

Let
\bm{y}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times 1}
be a vector that has
\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{i}
s as its elements, i.e.,
\bm{y}=[\bm{x}_{1}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{1},\ldots,\bm{x}_{k}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{k}]^{\top}
. Then we have:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}=\left\lVert\bm{X}\bm{y}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\bm{X}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{y}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

(A.106)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\bm{X}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{i})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\bm{X}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

(A.107)

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}\left\lVert\bm{X}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=C^{2}\left\lVert\bm{X}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{X}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C^{2}d\left\lVert\bm{X}\right\rVert_{2}^{4}

(A.108)

\displaystyle=C^{2}d\left\lVert\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}

(A.109)

\displaystyle=C^{2}d\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\,,

(A.110)

where Eq. (
A.106
) follows by the matrix operator norm inequality, Eq. (
A.107
) follows by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, Eq. (
A.108
) follows by
\left\lVert\bm{X}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{d}\left\lVert\bm{X}\right\rVert_{2}
, Eq. (
A.109
) follows from
\left\lVert\bm{X}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}=\left\lVert\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}\right\rVert_{2}
.
∎

The above result is tight. We can show that the lower bound of
\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}
under the conditions in the lemma is exactly
C\sqrt{d}\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\right\rVert_{2}
. Specifically, let
k=2
,
C=1
,
d=2
,
\bm{x}_{1}=[0,1]^{\top}
,
\bm{x}_{2}=[1,0]^{\top}
,
\bm{\theta}_{1}=[1,0]^{\top}
,
\bm{\theta}_{2}=[0,1]^{\top}
, then we have
\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{i}\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert[1,1]^{\top}\right\rVert_{2}=\sqrt{2}
, and
C\sqrt{d}\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{2}\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top}\right\rVert_{2}=1\times\sqrt{2}\times\left\lVert\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}\right\rVert_{2}=\sqrt{2}
. Therefore, we have that the upper bound given in Lemma
A.3.1
matches the lower bound.

We are now ready to prove the key Lemma
5.4.4
with the above Lemma
A.3.1
.

At any
t\geq T_{0}
, if the current partition is a “good partition", and
\overline{V}_{t}\notin\mathcal{V}
, then for all
\bm{x}_{a}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a}\right\rVert_{2}\leq 1
, with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}(\bm{\theta}_{i_{s}}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a}\right\rVert_{2}\left\lVert\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}(\bm{\theta}_{i_{s}}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}})\right\rVert_{2}

(A.111)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\right\rVert_{2}\left\lVert\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}(\bm{\theta}_{i_{s}}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}})\right\rVert_{2}

(A.112)

\displaystyle\leq 2\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{2d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}}\times\left\lVert\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\right\rVert_{2}\left\lVert\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}\right\rVert_{2}

(A.113)

\displaystyle\leq 2\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{2d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}}\times\frac{\lambda_{max}(\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top})}{\lambda_{\text{min}}(\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1})}

\displaystyle\leq 2\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{2d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}}\times\frac{T_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}}{2T_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}\tilde{\lambda}_{x}+\lambda}

(A.114)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{2d}}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\,,

where Eq.(
A.111
) follows by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, Eq.(
A.112
) follows from the inequality of matrix’s operator norm, Eq.(
A.113
) follows from the fact that in a “good partition",
\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}-\bm{\theta}_{l}\right\rVert_{2}\leq 2\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}},\forall{l\in\overline{V}_{t}}
and Lemma
A.3.1
, Eq.(
A.114
) follows by Eq.(
A.348
) with probability
\geq 1-\delta
.

A.3.8
Lemma
A.3.2
of the sufficient time
T_{0}
and its proof

The following lemma gives a sufficient time
T_{0}
for the algorithm to get a “good partition".

Lemma A.3.2
.

With the carefully designed edge deletion rule, after

\displaystyle T_{0}

\displaystyle\triangleq 16u\log(\frac{u}{\delta})+4u\max\max\{\frac{8d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}-\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}})^{2}}\log(\frac{u}{\delta}),\frac{16}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\log(\frac{8d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}\delta})\}

\displaystyle=O\bigg(u\left(\frac{d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}(\gamma_{1}-\zeta)^{2}}+\frac{1}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\right)\log\frac{1}{\delta}\bigg)

rounds, with probability at least
1-3\delta
for some
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{3})
, RCLUMB can always get a “good partition".

Below is the detailed proof of Lemma
A.3.2
.

Proof.

We first prove the following result:

With probability at least
1-\delta
for some
\delta\in(0,1)
, at any
t\in[T]
:

\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{\beta(T_{i,t},\frac{\delta}{u})+\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{T_{i,t}}}{\sqrt{\lambda+\lambda_{\text{min}}(\bm{M}_{i,t})}},\forall{i\in\mathcal{U}}\,,

(A.115)

where
\beta(T_{i,t},\frac{\delta}{u})\triangleq\sqrt{\lambda}+\sqrt{2\log(\frac{u}{\delta})+d\log(1+\frac{T_{i,t}}{\lambda d})}
.

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}+\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}\bigg(\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}(\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}+\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}+\eta_{s})\bigg)-\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}

(A.116)

\displaystyle=(\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}+\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}[(\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}+\lambda\bm{I})\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}-\lambda\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}+\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}]-\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}

\displaystyle=-\lambda\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}+\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}+\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}\,,

where we denote
\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}=\bm{M}_{i,t}+\lambda\bm{I}
, and Eq.(
A.116
) holds by definition.

Therefore,

\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\lambda\left\lVert\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}\right\rVert_{2}+\left\lVert\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}\right\rVert_{2}+\left\lVert\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}\right\rVert_{2}\,.

(A.117)

We then bound the three terms in Eq.(
A.162
) one by one. For the first term:

\lambda\left\lVert\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\lambda\left\lVert\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{\text{min}}(\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t})}}\,,

(A.118)

where we use the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, the inequality for the operator norm of matrices, and the fact that
\lambda_{\text{min}}(\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t})\geq\lambda
.

For the second term in Eq.(
A.162
):

\displaystyle\left\lVert\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle=\max_{\bm{x}\in S^{d-1}}\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bm{x}\in S^{d-1}}\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\left|\bm{x}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bm{x}\in S^{d-1}}\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\left|\bm{x}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\right|\left\lVert\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}\right\rVert_{\infty}

(A.119)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{*}\max_{\bm{x}\in S^{d-1}}\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\left|\bm{x}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{*}\max_{\bm{x}\in S^{d-1}}\sqrt{\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}1\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\left|\bm{x}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\right|^{2}}

(A.120)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{T_{i,t}}\sqrt{\max_{\bm{x}\in S^{d-1}}\bm{x}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}\bm{x}}

(A.121)

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{T_{i,t}}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{\text{min}}(\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t})}}\,,

(A.122)

where we denote
S^{d-1}=\{\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\left\lVert\bm{x}\right\rVert_{2}=1\}
, Eq.(
A.119
) follows from Holder’s inequality, Eq.(
A.120
) follows by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, Eq.(
A.121
) holds because
\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}\succeq\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}
, Eq.(
A.122
) follows from the Courant-Fischer theorem.

For the last term in Eq.(
A.162
)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}\right\rVert_{2}\left\lVert\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\rVert_{2}

(A.123)

\displaystyle=\frac{\left\lVert\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}\right\rVert_{\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{\text{min}}(\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t})}}\,,

(A.124)

where Eq.(
A.164
) follows by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and the inequality for the operator norm of matrices, and Eq.(
A.165
) follows by the Courant-Fischer theorem.

Following Theorem 1 in
[
1
]
, with probability at least
1-\delta
for some
\delta\in(0,1)
, for any
i\in\mathcal{U}
, we have:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}\right\rVert_{\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2\log(\frac{u}{\delta})+\log(\frac{\text{det}(\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t})}{\text{det}(\lambda\bm{I})})}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2\log(\frac{u}{\delta})+d\log(1+\frac{T_{i,t}}{\lambda d})}\,,

(A.125)

where
\text{det}(\bm{M})
denotes the determinant of matrix
\bm{M}
, Eq.(
A.166
) is because
\text{det}(\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t})\leq\Bigg(\frac{\text{trace}(\lambda\bm{I}+\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}=i}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top})}{d}\Bigg)^{d}\leq\big(\frac{\lambda d+T_{i,t}}{d}\big)^{d}
, and
\text{det}(\lambda\bm{I})=\lambda^{d}
.

Plugging Eq.(
A.166
) into Eq. (
A.165
), then plugging Eq. (
A.163
), Eq.(
A.122
) and Eq.(
A.165
) into Eq.(
A.162
), we can get that Eq.(
A.161
) holds with probability
\geq 1-\delta
.

Then, with the item regularity assumption stated in Assumption
9.4
, the technical Lemma
A.4.1
, together with Lemma 7 in
[
139
]
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, for a particular user
i
, at any
t
such that
T_{i,t}\geq\frac{16}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\log(\frac{8d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}\delta})
, we have:

\lambda_{\text{min}}(\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t})\geq 2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}T_{i,t}+\lambda\,.

(A.126)

Based on the above reasoning, we have: if
T_{i,t}\geq\frac{16}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\log(\frac{8d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}\delta})
, then with probability
\geq 1-2\delta
, we have:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta(T_{i,t},\frac{\delta}{u})+\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{T_{i,t}}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{\text{min}}(\tilde{\bm{M}}_{i,t})}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta(T_{i,t},\frac{\delta}{u})+\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{T_{i,t}}}{\sqrt{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}T_{i,t}+\lambda}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}+\sqrt{2\log(\frac{u}{\delta})+d\log(1+\frac{T_{i,t}}{\lambda d})}}{\sqrt{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}T_{i,t}+\lambda}}+\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}}\,,

(A.127)

for any
i\in\mathcal{U}
.

Let

\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}+\sqrt{2\log(\frac{u}{\delta})+d\log(1+\frac{T_{i,t}}{\lambda d})}}{\sqrt{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}T_{i,t}+\lambda}}+\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}}<\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}\,,

(A.128)

which is equivalent to

\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}+\sqrt{2\log(\frac{u}{\delta})+d\log(1+\frac{T_{i,t}}{\lambda d})}}{\sqrt{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}T_{i,t}+\lambda}}<\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}-\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}}\,,

(A.129)

where
\gamma_{1}
is given in Definition
5.2
.

Assume
\lambda\leq 2\log(\frac{u}{\delta})+d\log(1+\frac{T_{i,t}}{\lambda d})
, which is typically held, then a sufficient condition for Eq. (
A.129
) is:

\frac{2\log(\frac{u}{\delta})+d\log(1+\frac{T_{i,t}}{\lambda d})}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}T_{i,t}}<\frac{1}{4}(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}-\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}})^{2}\,.

(A.130)

To satisfy the condition in Eq.(
A.130
), it is sufficient to show

\frac{2\log(\frac{u}{\delta})}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}T_{i,t}}<\frac{1}{8}(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}-\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}})^{2}

(A.131)

and

\frac{d\log(1+\frac{T_{i,t}}{\lambda d})}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}T_{i,t}}<\frac{1}{8}(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}-\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}})^{2}\,.

(A.132)

From Eq.(
A.319
), we can get:

T_{i,t}\geq\frac{8\log(\frac{u}{\delta})}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}-\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}})^{2}}\,.

(A.133)

Following Lemma 9 in
[
139
]
, we can get the following sufficient condition for Eq.(
A.320
):

T_{i,t}\geq\frac{8d\log(\frac{4}{\lambda\tilde{\lambda}_{x}(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}-\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}})^{2}})}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}-\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}})^{2}}\,.

(A.134)

Assume
\frac{u}{\delta}\geq\frac{4}{\lambda\tilde{\lambda}_{x}(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}-\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}})^{2}}
, which is typically held, we can get that

T_{i,t}\geq\frac{8d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}-\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}})^{2}}\log(\frac{u}{\delta})

(A.135)

is a sufficient condition for Eq.(
A.128
). Together with the condition that
T_{i,t}\geq\frac{16}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\log(\frac{8d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}\delta})
, we can get that if

T_{i,t}\geq\max\{\frac{8d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}-\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}})^{2}}\log(\frac{u}{\delta}),\frac{16}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\log(\frac{8d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}\delta})\},\forall{i\in\mathcal{U}}\,,

(A.136)

then with probability
\geq 1-2\delta
:

\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}\right\rVert_{2}<\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}\,,\forall{i\in\mathcal{U}}\,.

By Lemma 8 in
[
139
]
, and Assumption
9.3
of user arrival uniformness, we have that for all

t\geq T_{0}\triangleq 16u\log(\frac{u}{\delta})+4u\max\{\frac{8d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}(\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}-\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}})^{2}}\log(\frac{u}{\delta}),\frac{16}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\log(\frac{8d}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}\delta})\}\,,

(A.137)

with probability at least
1-\delta
, condition in Eq.(
A.136
) is satisfied.

Therefore we have that for all
t\geq T_{0}
, with probability
\geq 1-3\delta
:

\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}\right\rVert_{2}<\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}\,,\forall{i\in\mathcal{U}}\,.

(A.138)

Next, we show that with Eq.(
A.325
), we can get that the RCLUMB keeps a “good partition". First, if we delete the edge
(i,l)
, then user
i
and user
j
belong to different
ground-truth clusters
, i.e.,
\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i}-\bm{\theta}_{l}\right\rVert_{2}>0
. This is because by the deletion rule of the algorithm, the concentration bound, and triangle inequality,

\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i}-\bm{\theta}_{l}\right\rVert_{2}=\left\lVert\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}-\bm{\theta}^{j(l)}\right\rVert_{2}\geq\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{l,t}\right\rVert_{2}-\left\lVert\bm{\theta}^{j(l)}-\bm{\theta}_{l,t}\right\rVert_{2}-\left\lVert\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}-\bm{\theta}_{i,t}\right\rVert_{2}>0
. Second, we show that if
\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i}-\bm{\theta}_{l}\right\rVert\geq\gamma_{1}>2\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}}
, the RCLUMB algorithm will delete the edge
(i,l)
. This is because if
\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i}-\bm{\theta}_{l}\right\rVert\geq\gamma_{1}
, then by the triangle inequality, and
\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}\right\rVert_{2}<\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}
,
\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{l,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(l)}\right\rVert_{2}<\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}
,
\bm{\theta}_{i}=\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}
,
\bm{\theta}_{l}=\bm{\theta}^{j(l)}
, we have
\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{l,t}\right\rVert_{2}\geq\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i}-\bm{\theta}_{l}\right\rVert-\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}\right\rVert_{2}-\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{l,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(l)}\right\rVert_{2}>\gamma_{1}-\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}-\frac{\gamma_{1}}{4}=\frac{\gamma_{1}}{2}>\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}+\sqrt{2\log(\frac{u}{\delta})+d\log(1+\frac{T_{i,t}}{\lambda d})}}{\sqrt{\lambda+2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}T_{i,t}}}+\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}}+\frac{\sqrt{\lambda}+\sqrt{2\log(\frac{u}{\delta})+d\log(1+\frac{T_{l,t}}{\lambda d})}}{\sqrt{\lambda+2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}T_{l,t}}}+\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{1}{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}}
, which will trigger the deletion condition Line
10
in Algo.
7
.

From the above reasoning, we can get that at round
t
, any user within
\overline{V}_{t}
is
\zeta
-close to
i_{t}
, and all the users belonging to
V_{j(i)}
are contained in
\overline{V}_{t}
, which means the algorithm has done a “good partition" at
t
by Definition
5.4
.
∎

A.3.9
Proof of Lemma
6.4.2

We prove the result in two situations: when
\overline{V}_{t}\in\mathcal{V}
and when
\overline{V}_{t}\notin\mathcal{V}
.

(1) Situation 1: for any
t\geq T_{0}
and
\overline{V}_{t}\in\mathcal{V}
, which means that the current user
i_{t}
is clustered completely correctly, i.e.,
\overline{V}_{t}=V_{j(i_{t})}
, therefore
\bm{\theta}_{l}=\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}},\forall{l\in\overline{V}_{t}}
, then we have:

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\ \bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}+\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}(\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}r_{s})-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\ \bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}+\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}\bigg(\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}(\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{i_{s}}+\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}+\eta_{s})\bigg)-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\ \bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}+\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}\bigg(\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}(\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}+\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}+\eta_{s})\bigg)-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\ \bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}+\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}[(\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}+\lambda\bm{I})\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}-\lambda\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}+\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}]-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}

\displaystyle=-\lambda\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}+\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}+\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}\,.

Therefore we have

\displaystyle\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}\right|+\left|\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}\right|\,.

(A.139)

Next, we bound the three terms in Eq.(
A.139
). For the first term:

\displaystyle\lambda\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}}\sqrt{\lambda_{\text{max}}(\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1})}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\sqrt{\lambda}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}}\,,

(A.140)

where we use the inequality of matrix norm, the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,
\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq 1
, and the fact that
\lambda_{\text{max}}(\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1})=\frac{1}{\lambda_{\text{min}}(\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1})}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}
.

For the second term in Eq.(
A.139
):

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\left\lVert\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}\right\rVert_{\infty}\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{*}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\right|\,,

(A.141)

where in the second inequality we use the Holder’s inequality.

For the last term, with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}}\left\lVert\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}}

(A.142)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}}\sqrt{2\log(\frac{1}{\delta})+\log(\frac{\text{det}(\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1})}{\text{det}(\lambda\bm{I})})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}}\sqrt{2\log(\frac{1}{\delta})+d\log(1+\frac{T}{\lambda d})}\,,

(A.143)

where the second inequality follows by Theorem 1 in
[
1
]
, Eq.(
A.143
) is because
\text{det}(\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1})\leq\Bigg(\frac{\text{trace}(\lambda\bm{I}+\sum_{s\in[t]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top})}{d}\Bigg)^{d}\leq\big(\frac{\lambda d+T_{\overline{V}_{t},t}}{d}\big)^{d}\leq\big(\frac{\lambda d+T}{d}\big)^{d}
, and
\text{det}(\lambda\bm{I})=\lambda^{d}
.

Plugging Eq.(
A.140
), Eq.(
A.141
) and Eq.(
A.143
) into Eq.(
A.139
), we can prove Lemma
6.4.2
in situation 1, i.e., for any
t\geq T_{0}
and
\overline{V}_{t}\in V
, with probability at least
1-\delta
:

\displaystyle\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{*}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\right|

\displaystyle+\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}}\bigg(\sqrt{\lambda}+\sqrt{2\log(\frac{1}{\delta})+d\log(1+\frac{T}{\lambda d})}\bigg)\,.

(A.144)

(2) Situation 2: for any
t\geq T_{0}
and
\overline{V}_{t}\notin\mathcal{V}
, which means that the current user is
misclustered
by the algorithm, i.e.,
\overline{V}_{t}\neq V_{j(i_{t})}
, but with Lemma
A.3.2
, with probability at least
1-3\delta
, the current partition is a “good partition", i.e.,
\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{l}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq 2\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{\frac{2}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}}},\forall{l\in\overline{V}_{t}}
, we have:

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\ \bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}+\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}(\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}r_{s})-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\ \bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}+\lambda\bm{I})^{-1}\bigg(\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}(\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{i_{s}}+\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}+\eta_{s})\bigg)-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}+\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}+\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}\bm{\theta}_{i_{s}}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}+\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}+\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}(\bm{\theta}_{i_{s}}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}(\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}+\lambda\bm{I})\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}-\lambda\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}

\displaystyle=\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}+\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}+\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}(\bm{\theta}_{i_{s}}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt-\lambda\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}\,.

Thus, with Lemma
5.4.4
and with the previous reasoning, with probability at least
1-5\delta
, we have:

\displaystyle\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}(\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}\right|+\left|\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{\epsilon}_{a_{s}}^{i_{s},s}\right|+\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\eta_{s}\right|

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}^{\top}(\bm{\theta}_{i_{s}}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{*}\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{V}_{t}}\left|\bm{x}_{a}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}\bm{x}_{a_{s}}\right|+\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{\overline{V}_{t},t-1}^{-1}}\bigg(\sqrt{\lambda}+\sqrt{2\log(\frac{1}{\delta})+d\log(1+\frac{T}{\lambda d})}\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{\epsilon_{*}\sqrt{2d}}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\,.

Therefore, combining situation 1 and situation 2, the result of Lemma
6.4.2
then follows.

A.3.10
Technical Lemmas and Their Proofs

We first prove the following technical lemma which is used to prove Lemma
A.3.2
.

Lemma A.3.3
.

Under Assumption
9.4
, at any time
t
, for any fixed unit vector
\bm{\theta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\mathbb{E}_{t}[(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{a_{t}})^{2}|\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|]\geq\tilde{\lambda}_{x}\triangleq\int_{0}^{\lambda_{x}}(1-e^{-\frac{(\lambda_{x}-x)^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}})^{C}dx\,.

(A.145)

Proof.

The proof of this lemma mainly follows the proof of Claim 1 in
[
81
]
, but with more careful analysis, since their assumption is more stringent than ours.

Denote the feasible arms at round
t
by
{\mathcal{A}_{t}=\{\bm{x}_{t,1},\bm{x}_{t,2},\ldots,\bm{x}_{t,\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|}\}}
.
Consider the corresponding i.i.d. random variables
\theta_{i}=(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{t,i})^{2}-\mathbb{E}_{t}[(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{t,i})^{2}|\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|],i=1,2,\ldots,\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|
. By Assumption
9.4
,
\theta_{i}
s are sub-Gaussian random variables with variance bounded by
\sigma^{2}
. Therefore, we have that for any
\alpha>0
and any
i\in[\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|]
:

\mathbb{P}_{t}(\theta_{i}<-\alpha|\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|)\leq e^{-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}}\,,

where
\mathbb{P}_{t}(\cdot)
is the shorthand for the conditional probability

\mathbb{P}(\cdot|(i_{1},\mathcal{A}_{1},r_{1}),\ldots,(i_{t-1},\mathcal{A}_{t-1},r_{t-1}),i_{t})
.

We also have that

\mathbb{E}_{t}[(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{t,i})^{2}|\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|=\mathbb{E}_{t}[\bm{\theta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{t,i}\bm{x}_{t,i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}|\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|]\geq\lambda_{\text{min}}(\mathbb{E}_{\bm{x}\sim\rho}[\bm{x}\bm{x}^{\top}])\geq\lambda_{x}
by Assumption
9.4
.
With the above inequalities, we can get

\mathbb{P}_{t}(\min_{i=1,\ldots,\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{t,i})^{2}\geq\lambda_{x}-\alpha|\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|)\geq(1-e^{-\frac{\alpha^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}})^{C}\,,

where
C
is the upper bound of
\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|
.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{t}[(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{a_{t}})^{2}|\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{t}[\min_{i=1,\ldots,\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{t,i})^{2}|\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|]

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}_{t}(\min_{i=1,\ldots,\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\bm{x}_{t,i})^{2}\geq x|\left|\mathcal{A}_{t}\right|)dx

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{\lambda_{x}}(1-e^{-\frac{(\lambda_{x}-x)^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}})^{C}dx\triangleq\tilde{\lambda}_{x}

∎

Finally, we prove the following lemma which is used in the proof of Theorem
6.4.3
.

Lemma A.3.4
.

\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\min\{.\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},t-1}^{-1}}^{2},1\}\leq 2d\log(1+\frac{T}{\lambda d}),\forall{j\in[m]}\,.

(A.146)

Proof.

\displaystyle\text{det}(\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},T})

\displaystyle=\text{det}\bigg(\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},T-1}+\mathbb{I}\{i_{T}\in V_{j}\}\bm{x}_{a_{T}}\bm{x}_{a_{T}}^{\top}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\text{det}(\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},T-1})\text{det}\bigg(\bm{I}+\mathbb{I}\{i_{T}\in V_{j}\}\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},T-1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bm{x}_{a_{T}}\bm{x}_{a_{T}}^{\top}\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},T-1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\text{det}(\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},T-1})\bigg(1+\mathbb{I}\{i_{T}\in V_{j}\}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{T}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},T-1}^{-1}}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\text{det}(\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},T_{0}})\prod_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\bigg(1+\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in V_{j}\}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},t-1}^{-1}}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\geq\text{det}(\lambda\bm{I})\prod_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\bigg(1+\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in V_{j}\}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},t-1}^{-1}}^{2}\bigg)\,.

(A.147)

\forall{x\in[0,1]}
, we have
x\leq 2\log(1+x)
. Therefore

\displaystyle\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\min\{\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in V_{j}\}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},t-1}^{-1}}^{2},1\}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\log\bigg(1+\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in V_{j}\}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},t-1}^{-1}}^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=2\log\bigg(\prod_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\big(1+\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in V_{j}\}\left\lVert\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\right\rVert_{\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},t-1}^{-1}}^{2}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 2[\log(\text{det}(\overline{\bm{M}}_{V_{j},T}))-\log(\text{det}(\lambda\bm{I}))]

\displaystyle\leq 2\log\bigg(\frac{\text{trace}(\lambda\bm{I}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in V_{j}\}\bm{x}_{a_{t}}\bm{x}_{a_{t}}^{\top})}{\lambda d}\bigg)^{d}

\displaystyle\leq 2d\log(1+\frac{T}{\lambda d})\,.

(A.148)

∎

A.3.11
Algorithms of RSCLUMB

This section introduces the Robust Set-based Clustering of Misspecified Bandits Algorithm (RSCLUMB). Unlike RCLUMB, which maintains a graph-based clustering structure, RSCLUMB maintains a set-based clustering structure. Besides, RCLUMB only splits clusters during the learning process, while RSCLUMB allows both split and merge operations. A brief illustration is that the agent will split a user out of its current set(cluster) if it finds an inconsistency between the user and its set, and if there are two clusters whose estimated preferences are close enough, the agent will merge them. A detailed discussion of the connection between the graph structure and the set structure can be found in
[
142
]
.

Now we introduce the details of RSCLUMB. The algorithm first initializes a single set
\bm{S}_{1}
containing all users and updates it during the learning process. The whole learning process consists of phases (Algo.
15
Line 3), where the
s-th
phase contains
2^{s}
rounds. At the beginning of each phase, the agent marks all users as "unchecked", and if a user comes later, it will be marked as "checked". If all users in a cluster are checked, then this cluster will be marked as "checked" meaning it is an accurate cluster in the current phase. With this mechanism, every phase can maintain an accuracy level, and the agent can put the accurate clusters aside and focus on exploring the inaccurate ones. For each cluster
V_{j}
, the algorithm maintains two estimated vectors
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{V_{j}}
and
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{V_{j}}
, where the
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{V_{j}}
is similar to the
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{\overline{V}_{j}}
in RCLUMB and is used for the recommendation, while the
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{V_{j}}
is the average of all the estimated user preference vectors in this cluster and is used for the split and merge operations.

At time
t
in phase
s
, the user
i_{\tau}
comes with the item set
