\displaystyle\geq\kappa_{\mu}(\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})^{\top}\kappa_{\mu}\bm{V}_{t-1}(\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})

\displaystyle=\kappa_{\mu}^{2}(\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})^{\top}\bm{V}_{t-1}(\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})

\displaystyle=\kappa_{\mu}^{2}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}_{\bm{V}_{t-1}}

\displaystyle\left(\text{as }||\bm{x}||_{\bm{A}}^{2}=\bm{x}^{\top}\bm{A}\bm{x}\right)

The first inequality is because
\bm{V}^{-1}_{t-1}\succeq\kappa_{\mu}\bm{M}^{-1}_{t-1}
, and the second inequality follows from
\bm{M}_{t-1}\succeq\kappa_{\mu}\bm{V}_{t-1}
.

Note that
\frac{\kappa_{\mu}}{\lambda}\mathbf{I}\succeq\bm{V}_{t-1}
, which allows us to show that

\begin{split}\left\lVert\lambda\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}=\lambda\sqrt{\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}^{\top}\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}}\leq\lambda\sqrt{\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}^{\top}\frac{\kappa_{\mu}}{\lambda}\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}}\leq\sqrt{\lambda\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\sqrt{\lambda\kappa_{\mu}}.\end{split}

(A.330)

Using the two equations above, we have that

\begin{split}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}}\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert G_{t}(\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})-\lambda\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}&\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert G_{t}(\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\lambda\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert G_{t}(\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}\end{split}

(A.331)

Then, let
\overline{f}_{t,s}=\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\tilde{\phi}_{s}
, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}^{2}}\left\lVert G_{t}(\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}^{2}}\left\lVert\sum_{s\in[t-1]:\atop i_{s}\in\overline{C}_{t}}(\mu(\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{\phi}_{s})-\mu(\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}^{\top}\widetilde{\phi}_{s}))\widetilde{\phi}_{s}+\lambda\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}^{2}}\left\lVert\sum_{s\in[t-1]:\atop i_{s}\in\overline{C}_{t}}(\mu(\overline{f}_{t,s})-\mu(\widetilde{f}_{i_{t},s}))\widetilde{\phi}_{s}+\lambda\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}^{2}}\left\lVert\sum_{s\in[t-1]:\atop i_{s}\in\overline{C}_{t}}(\mu(\overline{f}_{t,s})-(y_{s}-\epsilon_{s}))\widetilde{\phi}_{s}+\lambda\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\left(y_{s}=\mu(\widetilde{f}_{i_{t},s})+\epsilon_{s}\text{if }i_{s}=i_{t},\text{and}i_{s}=i_{t},\forall i_{s}\in\overline{C}_{t},\forall t\geq T_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}^{2}}\left\lVert\sum_{s\in[t-1]:\atop i_{s}\in\overline{C}_{t}}\left(\mu(\overline{f}_{t,s})-y_{s}\right)\widetilde{\phi}_{s}+\sum_{s\in[t-1]:\atop i_{s}\in\overline{C}_{t}}\epsilon_{s}\widetilde{\phi}_{s}+\lambda\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}^{2}}\left\lVert\sum_{s\in[t-1]:\atop i_{s}\in\overline{C}_{t}}\epsilon_{s}\widetilde{\phi}_{s}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}.

The last step holds due to the following reasoning. Recall that
\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}
is computed using MLE by solving the following equation:

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}

\displaystyle=\arg\min_{\bm{\theta}}-\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}\in\overline{C}_{t}}\left(y_{s}\log\mu\Big({\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\left[\phi(\bm{x}_{s,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{s,2})\right]\right)

\displaystyle+(1-y_{s})\log\mu\left({\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\left[\phi(\bm{x}_{s,2})-\phi(\bm{x}_{s,1})\right]\right)\Big)+\frac{1}{2}\lambda\left\lVert\bm{\theta}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}.

(A.332)

Setting its gradient to
0
, the following is satisfied:

\sum_{s\in[t-1]:\atop i_{s}\in\overline{C}_{t}}\left(\mu\left(\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{\phi}_{s}\right)-y_{s}\right)\widetilde{\phi}_{s}+\lambda\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}=0,

(A.333)

which is used in the last step.

Now we have

\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}}\leq\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\sum_{s\in[t-1]:\atop i_{s}\in\overline{C}_{t}}\epsilon_{s}\widetilde{\phi}_{s}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}.

(A.334)

Denote
\bm{V}\triangleq\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}\mathbf{I}
.
Note that the sequence of observation noises
\{\epsilon_{s}\}
is
1
-sub-Gaussian.

Next, we can apply Theorem 1 from
[
1
]
, to obtain

\left\lVert\sum_{s\in[t-1]:\atop i_{s}\in\overline{C}_{t}}\epsilon_{s}\widetilde{\phi}_{s}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}\leq 2\log\left(\frac{\det(\bm{V}_{t-1})^{1/2}}{\delta\det(\bm{V})^{1/2}}\right),

(A.335)

which holds with probability of at least
1-\delta
.

Next, based on our assumption that
\left\lVert\widetilde{\phi}_{s}\right\rVert_{2}\leq 2
, according to Lemma 10 from
[
1
]
, we have that

\det(\bm{V}_{t-1})\leq\left(\lambda/\kappa_{\mu}+4T/d\right)^{d}\,.

(A.336)

Therefore,

\sqrt{\frac{\det{\bm{V}_{t-1}}}{\det(V)}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\left(\lambda/\kappa_{\mu}+4T/d\right)^{d}}{(\lambda/\kappa_{\mu})^{d}}}=\left(1+4T\kappa_{\mu}/(d\lambda)\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}

(A.337)

This gives us

\left\lVert\sum_{s\in[t-1]:\atop i_{s}\in\overline{C}_{t}}\epsilon_{s}\widetilde{\phi}_{s}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}\leq 2\log\left(\frac{\det(\bm{V}_{t-1})^{1/2}}{\delta\det(V)^{1/2}}\right)\leq 2\log(1/\delta)+d\log\left(1+4T\kappa_{\mu}/(d\lambda)\right)

(A.338)

Combining all together, we have with probability at least
1-4\delta
for some
\delta\in(0,1/4)
, at any
t\geq T_{0}
:

\left\lVert\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{\lambda\kappa_{\mu}}+\sqrt{2\log(u/\delta)+d\log(1+4T\kappa_{\mu}/d\lambda)}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\,.

(A.339)

∎

Then, we prove the following lemma with the help of Lemma
A.8.3
.

Lemma A.8.4
.

For any iteration
t\geq T_{0}
, for all
\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{t}
, with probability of at least
1-4\delta
, we have

|\left(f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x})-f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x}^{\prime})\right)-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\left(\phi(\bm{x})-\phi(\bm{x}^{\prime})\right)|\leq\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x})-\phi(\bm{x}^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\,,

where
\beta_{T}=\sqrt{\lambda\kappa_{\mu}}+\sqrt{2\log(u/\delta)+d\log(1+4T\kappa_{\mu}/d\lambda)}
.

Proof.

\begin{split}&|\left(f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x})-f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x}^{\prime})\right)-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\left(\phi(\bm{x})-\phi(\bm{x}^{\prime})\right)|\\
&=|\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}^{\top}\left[(\phi(\bm{x})-\phi(\bm{x}^{\prime})\right]-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\left[\phi(\bm{x})-\phi(\bm{x}^{\prime})\right]|\\
&=|\left(\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\right)^{\top}\left[\phi(\bm{x})-\phi(\bm{x}^{\prime})\right]|\\
&\leq\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{i_{t}}-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x})-\phi(\bm{x}^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\\
&\leq\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x})-\phi(\bm{x}^{\prime})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}},\end{split}

in which the last inequality follows from Lemma
A.8.3
.
∎

We also prove the following lemma to upper bound the summation of squared norms which will be used in proving the final regret bound.

Lemma A.8.5
.

With probability at least
1-4\delta
, we have

\sum^{T}_{t=T_{0}}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in C_{j}\}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}\leq 2d\log\left(1+4T\kappa_{\mu}/(d\lambda)\right)\,,\forall j\in[m]\,,

where
\mathbb{I}
denotes the indicator function.

Proof.

We denote
\widetilde{\phi}_{t}=\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})
.
Recall that we have assumed that
\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{2}\leq 2
.
It is easy to verify that
\bm{V}_{t-1}\succeq\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}I
and hence
\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}\preceq\frac{\kappa_{\mu}}{\lambda}I
.
Therefore, we have that
\left\lVert\widetilde{\phi}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}\leq\frac{\kappa_{\mu}}{\lambda}\left\lVert\widetilde{\phi}_{t}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{4\kappa_{\mu}}{\lambda}
. We choose
\lambda
such that
\frac{4\kappa_{\mu}}{\lambda}\leq 1
, which ensures that
\left\lVert\widetilde{\phi}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}\leq 1
.
Our proof here mostly follows from Lemma 11 of
[
1
]
and Lemma J.2 of
[
236
]
. To begin with, note that
x\leq 2\log(1+x)
for
x\in[0,1]
. Denote
\bm{V}_{t,j}=\sum_{s\in[t-1]:\atop i_{s}\in C_{j}}\widetilde{\phi}_{s}\widetilde{\phi}_{s}^{\top}+\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}\mathbf{I}
. Then we have that

\begin{split}\sum^{T}_{t=T_{0}}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in C_{j}\}\left\lVert\widetilde{\phi}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}&\leq\sum^{T}_{t=T_{0}}2\log\left(1+\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in C_{j}\}\left\lVert\widetilde{\phi}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}\right)\\
&=2\left(\log\det V_{T,j}-\log\det V\right)\\
&=2\log\frac{\det V_{T,j}}{\det V}\\
&\leq 2\log\left(\left(1+4T\kappa_{\mu}/(d\lambda)\right)^{d}\right)\\
&=2d\log\left(1+4T\kappa_{\mu}/(d\lambda)\right).\end{split}

(A.340)

The second inequality follows the same reasoning as (
A.337
).
This completes the proof.
∎

Now we are ready to prove Theorem
9.4.1
.
First, we have

R_{T}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}r_{t}\leq T_{0}+\sum_{t=T_{0}}^{T}r_{t}\,,

(A.341)

where we use that the reward at each round is bounded by 1.

Then, we only need to upper bound the regret after
T_{0}
. By Lemma
A.8.2
, we know that with probability at least
1-4\delta
, the algorithm can cluster all the users correctly,
\overline{C}_{t}=C_{j(i_{t})}
, and the statements of all the above lemmas hold. We have that for any
t\geq T_{0}
:

\begin{split}r_{t}&=f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t,1})+f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-f_{i_{t}}(x_{t,2})\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\left(\phi(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right)+\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\left(\phi(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right)\\
&+\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\\
&=\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\left(\phi(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right)+\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+\\
&\hskip 23.49976pt\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\left(\phi(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right)+\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\left(\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right)+\\
&\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})+\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}2\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\left(\phi(x^{*})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right)+2\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\phi(x^{*})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+\\
&\hskip 23.49976pt\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\left(\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right)+\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}2\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\left(\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right)+2\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+\\
&\hskip 23.49976pt\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\left(\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right)+\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\\
&\leq\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\left(\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right)+3\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{\leq}}3\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\\
\end{split}

Step
(a)
follows from Lemma
A.8.4
. Step
(b)
makes use of the triangle inequality.
Step
(c)
follows from the way in which we choose the second arm
\bm{x}_{t,2}
:
\bm{x}_{t,2}=\arg\max_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{t}}\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\left(\phi(x)-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right)+\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\left\lVert\phi(x)-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}
.
Step
(d)
results from the way in which we select the first arm:
\bm{x}_{t,1}=\arg\max_{x\in\mathcal{X}_{t}}\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}^{\top}\phi(x)
.

Then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=T_{0}}^{T}r_{t}

\displaystyle\leq 3\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\sum_{t=T_{0}}^{T}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}

\displaystyle=3\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\sum_{t=T_{0}}^{T}\sum_{j\in[m]}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in C_{j}\}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq 3\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\sqrt{\sum_{t=T_{0}}^{T}\sum_{j\in[m]}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in C_{j}\}\sum_{t=T_{0}}^{T}\sum_{j\in[m]}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in C_{j}\}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 3\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\sqrt{T\cdot m\cdot 2d\log\left(1+4T\kappa_{\mu}/(d\lambda)\right)}\,,

(A.342)

where in the second inequality we use the Cauchy-Swarchz inequality, and in the last step we use
\sum_{t=T_{0}}^{T}\sum_{j\in[m]}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in C_{j}\}\leq T
and Lemma
A.8.5
.

Therefore, finally, we have with probability at least
1-4\delta

\displaystyle R_{T}

\displaystyle\leq T_{0}+3\frac{\beta_{T}}{\kappa_{\mu}}\sqrt{T\cdot m\cdot 2d\log\left(1+4T\kappa_{\mu}/(d\lambda)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq O(u(\frac{d}{\kappa_{\mu}^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{x}\gamma^{2}}+\frac{1}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}})\log T+\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}}d\sqrt{mT})

\displaystyle=O(\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}}d\sqrt{mT})\,,

(A.343)

A.9
Proof of Theorem
9.4.2

A.9.1
Auxiliary Definitions and Explanations

Denifition of the NTK matrix
\mathbf{H}_{j}
for cluster
j
.

Recall that we use
T_{j}
to denote the total number of iterations in which the users in cluster
j
are served.
For cluster
j
, let
\{x_{(i)}\}_{i=1}^{T_{j}K}
be a set of all
T_{j}\times K
possible arm feature vectors:
\{x_{t,a}\}_{1\leq t\leq T_{j},1\leq a\leq K}
, where
i=K(t-1)+a
.
Firstly, we define
\mathbf{h}_{t}=[f^{j}(x_{(i)})]_{i=1,\ldots,T_{j}K}^{\top}
, i.e.,
\mathbf{h}_{t}
is the
T_{j}K
-dimensional vector containing the reward function values of the arms corresponding to cluster
j
.
Next, define

\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{p,q}^{(1)}=\mathbf{\Sigma}_{p,q}^{(1)}=\langle x_{(p)},x_{(q)}\rangle,\\
\mathbf{A}_{p,q}^{(l)}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{\Sigma}_{p,q}^{(l)}&\mathbf{\Sigma}_{p,q}^{(l)}&\\
\mathbf{\Sigma}_{p,q}^{(l)}&\mathbf{\Sigma}_{q,q}^{(l)}&\end{pmatrix},

\mathbf{\Sigma}_{p,q}^{(l+1)}=2\mathbb{E}_{(u,v)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbf{A}_{p,q}^{(l)})}[\max\{u,0\}\max\{v,0\}],

\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{p,q}^{(l+1)}=2\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}_{p,q}^{(l)}\mathbb{E}_{(u,v)\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbf{A}_{p,q}^{(l)})}[\mathbbm{1}(u\geq 0)\mathbbm{1}(v\geq 0)]+\mathbf{\Sigma}_{p,q}^{(l+1)}.

With these definitions, the NTK matrix for cluster
j
is then defined as
\mathbf{H}_{j}=(\widetilde{\mathbf{H}}^{(L)}+\mathbf{\Sigma}^{(L)})/2
.

The Initial Parameters
\bm{\theta}_{0}
.

Next, we discuss how the initial parameters
\bm{\theta}_{0}
are obtained.
We adopt the same initialization method from
[
275
,
291
]
.
Specifically, for each
l=1,\ldots,L-1
, let

\mathbf{W}_{l}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathbf{W}&\mathbf{0}\\
\mathbf{0}&\mathbf{W}\end{array}\right)

in which every entry of
\mathbf{W}
is independently and randomly sampled from
\mathcal{N}(0,4/m_{\text{NN}})
, and choose
\mathbf{W}_{L}=(\mathbf{w}^{\top},-\mathbf{w}^{\top})
in which every entry of
\mathbf{w}
is independently and randomly sampled from
\mathcal{N}(0,2/m_{\text{NN}})
.

Justifications for Assumption
9.5
.

The last assumption in Assumption
9.5
, together with the way we initialize
\theta_{0}
as discussed above, ensures that the initial output of the NN is
0
:
h(x;\theta_{0})=0,\forall x\in\mathcal{X}
.
The assumption of
x_{j}=x_{j+d/2}
from Assumption
9.5
is a mild assumption which is commonly adopted by previous works on neural bandits
[
291
,
275
]
.
To ensure that this assumption holds, for any arm
x
, we can always firstly normalize it such that
||x||=1
, and then construct a new context
x^{\prime}=(x^{\top},x^{\top})^{\top}/\sqrt{2}
to satisfy this assumption
[
291
]
.

A.9.2
Proof

To begin with, we first list the specific conditions we need for the width
m_{\text{NN}}
of the NN:

\begin{split}&m_{\text{NN}}\geq CT^{4}K^{4}L^{6}\log(T^{2}K^{2}L/\delta)/\lambda_{0}^{4},\\
&m_{\text{NN}}(\log m)^{-3}\geq C\kappa_{\mu}^{-3}T^{8}L^{21}\lambda^{-5},\\
&m_{\text{NN}}(\log m_{\text{NN}})^{-3}\geq C\kappa_{\mu}^{-3}T^{14}L^{21}\lambda^{-11}L_{\mu}^{6},\\
&m_{\text{NN}}(\log m_{\text{NN}})^{-3}\geq CT^{14}L^{18}\lambda^{-8},\end{split}

(A.344)

for some absolute constant
C>0
.
To ease exposition, we express these conditions above as

m_{\text{NN}}\geq\text{poly}(T,L,K,1/\kappa_{\mu},L_{\mu},1/\lambda_{0},1/\lambda,\log(1/\delta))
.

In our proof here, we use the gradient of the NN at
\bm{\theta}_{0}
to derive the feature mapping for the arms, i.e., we let
\phi(\bm{x})=g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})/\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}
.
We use
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}
to denote the paramters of the NN after training in iteration
t
(see Algorithm
19
).

We use the following lemma to show that for every cluster
j\in\mathcal{C}
, its reward function
f^{j}
can be expressed as a linear function with respect to the initial gradient
g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})
.

Lemma A.9.1

(Lemma B.3 of
[
275
]
)
.

As long as the width
m
of the NN is large enough:

m_{\text{NN}}\geq C_{0}T^{4}K^{4}L^{6}\log(T^{2}K^{2}L/\delta)/\lambda_{0}^{4},

then for all clusters
j\in[m]
, with probability of at least
1-\delta
, there exits a
\bm{\theta}^{j}_{f}
such that

f^{j}(\bm{x})=\langle g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\bm{\theta}^{j}_{f}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle,\hskip 23.49976pt\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}^{j}_{f}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\sqrt{2\mathbf{h}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{H}_{j}^{-1}\mathbf{h}_{j}}\leq B.

for all
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{t}
,
t\in[T]
with
i_{t}\in C_{j}
.

Lemma
A.9.1
is the formal statement of Lemma
9.2.1
from Sec.
9.2.2
.
Note that the constant
B
is applicable to all
m
clusters.

The following lemma converts our assumption about cluster separation (Assumption
9.6
) into the difference between the linearized parameters for different clusters.

Lemma A.9.2
.

If users
i
and
l
belong to different clusters, then we have that

\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{f,i}-\bm{\theta}_{f,l}\right\rVert\geq\gamma^{\prime}.

Proof.

To begin with, Lemma
A.9.1
tells us that

\begin{split}|f_{i}(\bm{x})-f_{l}(\bm{x})|=|\langle g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\bm{\theta}_{f,i}-\bm{\theta}_{f,l}\rangle|\leq\left\lVert g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})\right\rVert\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{f,i}-\bm{\theta}_{f,l}\right\rVert.\end{split}

(A.345)

This leads to

\begin{split}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{f,i}-\bm{\theta}_{f,l}\right\rVert\geq\frac{|f_{i}(\bm{x})-f_{l}(\bm{x})|}{\left\lVert g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})\right\rVert}\geq\frac{\gamma^{\prime}}{\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}},\end{split}

(A.346)

in which we have made use of Assumption
9.6
and our assumption that
\frac{1}{m_{\text{NN}}}\langle g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0}),g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})\rangle\leq 1
in the last inequality.
This completes the proof.
∎

The following lemma shows that for every user, the output of the NN trained using its own local data can be approximated by a linear function.

Lemma A.9.3
.

Let
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},t}\triangleq C_{2}m_{\text{NN}}^{-1/6}\sqrt{\log m_{\text{NN}}}L^{3}\left(\frac{t}{\lambda}\right)^{4/3}
where
C_{2}>0
is an absolute constant.
Then

|\langle g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle-h(\bm{x};\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t})|\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},t},\,\,\,\forall t\in[T],\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{t}.

Proof.

This lemma can be proved following a similar line of proof as Lemma 1 from
[
221
]
.
Here the
t
in
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},t}
can in fact be replaced by
T_{i,t}\leq t
, however, we have simply used its upper bound
t
for simplicity.
∎

Lemma A.9.4
.

Let
\beta_{T}\triangleq\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}}\sqrt{\widetilde{d}+2\log(u/\delta)}
.
Assuming that the conditions on
m_{\text{NN}}
from
eq.

A.344
are satisfied.
With probability of at least
1-\delta
, we have that

\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{f,i}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{\beta_{T}+B\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}+1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{V}_{i,t-1})}},\hskip 23.49976pt\forall t\in[T].

where
\bm{V}_{i,t-1}=\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}\mathbf{I}+\sum_{s\in[t-1]\atop i_{s}=i}(\phi(\bm{x}_{s,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{s,2}))(\phi(\bm{x}_{s,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{s,2}))^{\top}
,
\phi(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}}g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})
, and
T_{i,t}
denotes the number of rounds of seeing user
i
in the first
t
rounds.

Proof.

In iteration
t
, for any user
i\in\mathcal{U}
, the user leverages its current history of observations
\{(\bm{x}_{s,1},\bm{x}_{s,2},y_{s})\}_{s\in[t-1],i_{s}=i}
to train the NN by minimizing the loss function ((
A.299
)), to obtain the NN parameters
\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}
.
Note that the NN has been trained when the most recent observation in
\{(\bm{x}_{s,1},\bm{x}_{s,2},y_{s})\}_{s\in[t-1],i_{s}=i}
was collected, i.e., the last time when user
i
was encountered.
Of note, according to Lemma
A.9.1
, the latent reward function of user
i
can be expressed as
f_{i}(\bm{x})=\langle g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\bm{\theta}_{f,i}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle
.
Therefore, from the perspective of each individual user
i
, the user is faced with a
neural dueling bandit
problem instance.
As a result, we can modifying the proof of Lemma 6 from
[
221
]
to show that
with probability of at least
1-\delta
,

\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{f,i}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{i,t-1}}\leq\beta_{T}+B\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}+1,\hskip 23.49976pt\forall t\in[T],i\in\mathcal{U}.

Here in our definition of
\beta_{T}\triangleq\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}}\sqrt{\widetilde{d}+2\log(u/\delta)}
, we have replaced the error probability
\delta
(from
[
221
]
) by
\delta/u
to account for the use of an extra union bound over all
u
users.

This allows us to show that

\begin{split}\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{f,i}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}\right\rVert_{2}&\leq\frac{\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{f,i}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{i,t-1}}}{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{V}_{i,t-1})}}\\
&\leq\frac{\beta_{T}+B\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}+1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{V}_{i,t-1})}}\end{split}

(A.347)

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma A.9.5
.

With the carefully designed edge deletion rule in Algorithm
19
, after

\displaystyle T_{0}

\displaystyle\triangleq 16u\log(\frac{u}{\delta})+4u\max\left\{\frac{32\left(\widetilde{d}+2\log(u/\delta)\right)}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}\gamma^{2}\kappa_{\mu}^{2}},\frac{16}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\log(\frac{24udm^{2}(L-1)}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}\delta})\right\}

\displaystyle=O\left(u\left(\frac{\widetilde{d}}{\kappa_{\mu}^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{x}\gamma^{2}}+\frac{1}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\right)\log(\frac{1}{\delta})\right),

rounds, with probability at least
1-3\delta
for some
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{3})
, CONDB can cluster all the users correctly.

Proof.

Recall that we use
p=dm_{\text{NN}}+m_{\text{NN}}^{2}(L-1)+m_{\text{NN}}
to denote the total number of parameters of the NN.
Similar to the proof of Lemma
A.8.2
, with the item regularity assumption stated in Assumption
9.4
, Lemma J.1 in
[
236
]
, together with Lemma 7 in
[
139
]
(note that when using these technical results, we use
g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta})/\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}
as the feature vector to replace the original feature vector of
\bm{x}
), and applying a union bound, with probability at least
1-\delta
, for all
i\in\mathcal{U}
, at any
t
such that
T_{i,t}\geq\frac{16}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\log(\frac{8up}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}\delta})
, we have:

\lambda_{\text{min}}(\bm{V}_{i,t})\geq 2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}T_{i,t}\,.

(A.348)

Note that compared with the proof of
A.8.2
, in the lower bound on
T_{i,t}
here, we have replaced the dimension
d
by
p
. This has led to a logarithmic dependence
on the width
m_{\text{NN}}
of the NN.
To simplify the exposition, using the fact that
p\geq 3dm_{\text{NN}}^{2}(L-1)
, we replace this condition on
T_{i,t}
by a slightly stricter condition:
T_{i,t}\geq\frac{16}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\log(\frac{8u\times 3dm_{\text{NN}}^{2}(L-1)}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}\delta})=\frac{16}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\log(\frac{24udm_{\text{NN}}^{2}(L-1)}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}\delta})
.

Then, together with Lemma
A.9.4
, we have: if

T_{i,t}\geq\frac{16}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\log(\frac{8u\times 3dm_{\text{NN}}^{2}(L-1)}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}\delta})
,
then with probability
\geq 1-2\delta
, we have:

\displaystyle\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\beta_{T}+B\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}+1}{\sqrt{\lambda_{\min}(\bm{V}_{i,t-1})}}\leq\frac{\beta_{T}+B\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}+1}{\sqrt{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}T_{i,t}}}\,.

Now, let

\frac{\beta_{T}+B\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}+1}{\sqrt{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}T_{i,t}}}<\frac{\gamma}{4}\,,

(A.349)

Note that in Algorithm
19
, we have defined the funciton
f
as

f(T_{i,t})\triangleq\frac{\beta_{T}+B\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}+1}{\sqrt{2\tilde{\lambda}_{x}T_{i,t}}}

(A.350)

This immediately leads to

\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}\right\rVert\leq f(T_{i,t})<\frac{\gamma}{4}.

(A.351)

For simplicity, now let
B\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}+1\leq\beta_{T}
which is typically satisfied. This allows us to show that

T_{i,t}>\frac{32\beta_{T}^{2}}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}\gamma^{2}}=\frac{32\left(\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}}\sqrt{\widetilde{d}+2\log(u/\delta)}\right)^{2}}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}\gamma^{2}}=\frac{32\left(\widetilde{d}+2\log(u/\delta)\right)}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}\gamma^{2}\kappa_{\mu}^{2}}.

(A.352)

Combining both conditions on
T_{i,t}
together, we have that

T_{i,t}\geq\max\left\{\frac{32\left(\widetilde{d}+2\log(u/\delta)\right)}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}\gamma^{2}\kappa_{\mu}^{2}},\frac{16}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\log(\frac{24udm_{\text{NN}}^{2}(L-1)}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}\delta})\right\}

(A.353)

By Lemma 8 in
[
139
]
and Assumption
9.3
of user arrival uniformness, we have that for all

\displaystyle T_{0}

\displaystyle\triangleq 16u\log(\frac{u}{\delta})+4u\max\left\{\frac{32\left(\widetilde{d}+2\log(u/\delta)\right)}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}\gamma^{2}\kappa_{\mu}^{2}},\frac{16}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\log(\frac{24udm_{\text{NN}}^{2}(L-1)}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}\delta})\right\}

\displaystyle=O\left(u\left(\frac{\widetilde{d}}{\kappa_{\mu}^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{x}\gamma^{2}}+\frac{1}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}}\right)\log(\frac{1}{\delta})\right),

the condition in Eq.(
A.352
) is satisfied with probability at least
1-\delta
.

Therefore we have that for all
t\geq T_{0}
, with probability
\geq 1-3\delta
:

\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}\right\rVert_{2}<\frac{\gamma}{4}\,,\forall{i\in\mathcal{U}}\,.

(A.354)

Finally, we show that as long as the condition
\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}\right\rVert_{2}<\frac{\gamma}{4}\,,\forall{i\in\mathcal{U}}
, our algorithm can cluster all the users correctly.

First, we show that when the edge
(i,l)
is deleted, user
i
and user
j
must belong to different
ground-truth clusters
, i.e.,
\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{f,i}-\bm{\theta}_{f,l}\right\rVert_{2}>0
.
This is because by the deletion rule of the algorithm, the concentration bound, and triangle inequality

\displaystyle\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{f,i}-\bm{\theta}_{f,l}\right\rVert_{2}=\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}-\bm{\theta}^{j(l)}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{l,t}\right\rVert_{2}-\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}^{j(l)}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{l,t}\right\rVert_{2}-\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{l,t}\right\rVert_{2}-f(T_{i,t})-f(T_{l,t})>0\,.

(A.355)

Second, we can show that if

|f_{i}(\bm{x})-f_{l}(\bm{x})|\geq\gamma^{\prime},\forall\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}
,
meaning that user
i
and user
l
are not in the same
ground-truth cluster
, CONDB will delete the edge
(i,l)
after
T_{0}
.
Note that when user
i
and user
l
are not in the same
ground-truth cluster
, Lemma
A.9.2
tells us that
\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{f,i}-\bm{\theta}_{f,l}\right\rVert\geq\gamma^{\prime}
.
Then we have that

\displaystyle\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{l,t}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\geq\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{f,i}-\bm{\theta}_{f,l}\right\rVert-\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{i,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(i)}\right\rVert_{2}-\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\hat{\bm{\theta}}_{l,t}-\bm{\theta}^{j(l)}\right\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle>\gamma-\frac{\gamma}{4}-\frac{\gamma}{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{2}>f(T_{i,t})+f(T_{l,t})\,,

(A.356)

which will trigger the edge deletion rule to delete edge
(i,l)
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Then, we prove the following lemmas for the cluster-based statistics.

Lemma A.9.6
.

Assuming that the conditions on
m
from
eq.

A.344
are satisfied.
With probability at least
1-4\delta
for some
\delta\in(0,1/4)
, at any
t\geq T_{0}
:

\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{f,i_{t}}-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}}\leq\beta_{T}+B\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}+1,\hskip 23.49976pt\forall t\in[T].

Proof.

To begin with, note that by Lemma
A.9.5
, we have that with probability of at least
1-3\delta
, all users are clustered correctly, i.e.,
\overline{C}_{t}=C_{j(i_{t})},\forall t\geq T_{0}
.
Note that according to our Algorithm
19
, in iteration
t
, we select the pair of arms using all the data collected by all users in cluster
\overline{C}_{t}
.
That is,
\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}
represents the NN parameters trained using the data from all users in the cluster
\overline{C}_{t}
(i.e.,
\{(\bm{x}_{s,1},\bm{x}_{s,2},y_{s})\}_{s\in[t-1],i_{s}\in\overline{C}_{t}}
), and
\bm{V}_{t}
also contains the data from all users in this cluster
\overline{C}_{t}
.
Therefore, in iteration
t
, we are effectively following a neural dueling bandit algorithm using
\{(\bm{x}_{s,1},\bm{x}_{s,2},y_{s})\}_{s\in[t-1],i_{s}\in\overline{C}_{t}}
as the current observation history.
This allows us to leverage the proof of Lemma 6 from
[
221
]
to complete the proof.
∎

Lemma A.9.7
.

Let
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},t}\triangleq C_{2}m_{\text{NN}}^{-1/6}\sqrt{\log m_{\text{NN}}}L^{3}\left(\frac{t}{\lambda}\right)^{4/3}
where
C_{2}>0
is an absolute constant.
Then

|\langle g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}^{\prime};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle-(h(\bm{x};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})-h(\bm{x}^{\prime};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}))|\leq 2\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},t},\,\,\,\forall t\in[T],\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{t}.

Proof.

This lemma can be proved following a similar line of proof as Lemma 1 from
[
221
]
.
∎

Lemma A.9.8
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
,
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},t}\doteq C_{2}m_{\text{NN}}^{-1/6}\sqrt{\log m_{\text{NN}}}L^{3}\left(\frac{t}{\lambda}\right)^{4/3}
for some
C_{2}>0
.
As long as
m_{\text{NN}}\geq\text{poly}(T,L,K,u,1/\kappa_{\mu},L_{\mu},1/\lambda_{0},1/\lambda,\log(1/\delta))
, then with probability of at least
1-\delta
, at any
t\geq T_{0}
,

|\left[f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x})-f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x}^{\prime})\right]-\left[h(\bm{x};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})-h(\bm{x}^{\prime};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right]|\leq\nu_{T}\sigma_{t-1}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime})+2\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},t},

for all
\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{t},t\in[T]
.

Proof.

Denote
\phi(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}}g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})
.
Recall that
lemma

A.9.1
tells us that
f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x})=\langle g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\bm{\theta}_{f,i_{t}}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle=\langle\phi(\bm{x}),\bm{\theta}_{f,i_{t}}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle
for all
\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{t},t\in[T]
.
To begin with, for all
\bm{x},\bm{x}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}_{t},t\in[T]
we have that

\begin{split}|&f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x})-f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x}^{\prime})-\langle g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}^{\prime};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle|\\
&=|\langle g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}^{\prime};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\bm{\theta}_{f,i_{t}}-\theta_{0}\rangle-\langle g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}^{\prime};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle|\\
&=|\langle g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}^{\prime};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\bm{\theta}_{f,i_{t}}-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\rangle\rangle|\\
&=|\langle\phi(\bm{x})-\phi(\bm{x}^{\prime}),\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left(\bm{\theta}_{f,i_{t}}-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\right)\rangle|\\
&\leq\left\lVert\left(\phi(\bm{x})-\phi(\bm{x}^{\prime})\right)\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}\left\lVert\bm{\theta}_{f,i_{t}}-\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}}\\
&\leq\left\lVert\left(\phi(\bm{x})-\phi(\bm{x}^{\prime})\right)\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\left(\beta_{T}+B\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}+1\right),\end{split}

(A.357)

in which we have used Lemma
A.9.6
in the last inequality.
Now making use of the equation above and
lemma

A.9.7
, we have that

\begin{split}&|f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x})-f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x}^{\prime})-(h(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{t})-h(\bm{x}^{\prime};\bm{\theta}_{t}))|\\
&=|f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x})-f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x}^{\prime})-\langle g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}^{\prime};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle\\
&\hskip 23.49976pt\hskip 23.49976pt\hskip 23.49976pt+\langle g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}^{\prime};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle-(h(\bm{x};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})-h(\bm{x}^{\prime};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}))|\\
&\leq|f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x})-f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x}^{\prime})-\langle g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}^{\prime};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle|\\
&\hskip 23.49976pt\hskip 23.49976pt\hskip 23.49976pt+|\langle g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}^{\prime};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle-(h(\bm{x};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})-h(\bm{x}^{\prime};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}))|\\
&\leq\left\lVert\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}}\left(\phi(\bm{x})-\phi(\bm{x}^{\prime})\right)\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\left(\beta_{T}+B\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}+1\right)+2\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},t}.\\
\end{split}

(A.358)

This completes the proof.
∎

We also prove the following lemma to upper bound the summation of squared norms which will be used in proving the final regret bound.

Lemma A.9.9
.

With probability at least
1-4\delta
, we have

\sum^{T}_{t=T_{0}}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in C_{j}\}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}\leq 16\widetilde{d}\,,\forall j\in[m]\,,

where
\mathbb{I}
denotes the indicator function.

Proof.

We denote
\widetilde{\phi}_{t}=\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})
.
Note that we have defined
\phi(\bm{x})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}}g(\bm{x};\bm{\theta}_{0})
.
Here we assume that
\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{2}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{m_{\text{NN}}}}\left\lVert g(\bm{x}_{t,1};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}_{t,2};\bm{\theta}_{0})\right\rVert_{2}\leq 2
.
Replacing
2
by an absolute constant
c_{0}
would only change the final regret bound by a constant factor, so we omit it for simplicity.

It is easy to verify that
\bm{V}_{t-1}\succeq\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}I
and hence
\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}\preceq\frac{\kappa_{\mu}}{\lambda}I
.
Therefore, we have that
\left\lVert\widetilde{\phi}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}\leq\frac{\kappa_{\mu}}{\lambda}\left\lVert\widetilde{\phi}_{t}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{4\kappa_{\mu}}{\lambda}
. We choose
\lambda
such that
\frac{4\kappa_{\mu}}{\lambda}\leq 1
, which ensures that
\left\lVert\widetilde{\phi}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}\leq 1
.
Our proof here mostly follows from Lemma 11 of
[
1
]
and Lemma J.2 of
[
236
]
. To begin with, note that
x\leq 2\log(1+x)
for
x\in[0,1]
. Denote
\bm{V}_{t,j}=\sum_{s\in[t-1]:\atop i_{s}\in C_{j}}\widetilde{\phi}_{s}\widetilde{\phi}_{s}^{\top}+\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}\mathbf{I}
. Then we have that

\begin{split}\sum^{T}_{t=T_{0}}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in C_{j}\}\left\lVert\widetilde{\phi}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}&\leq\sum^{T}_{t=T_{0}}2\log\left(1+\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in C_{j}\}\left\lVert\widetilde{\phi}_{t}\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}\right)\\
&\leq 16\log\det\left(\frac{\kappa_{\mu}}{\lambda}\mathbf{H}^{\prime}+\mathbf{I}\right)\\
&\triangleq 16\widetilde{d}.\end{split}

(A.359)

The second inequality follows from the proof in Section A.3 from
[
221
]
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Now we are ready to prove Theorem
9.4.2
.
To begin with, we have that

R_{T}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}r_{t}\leq T_{0}+\sum_{t=T_{0}}^{T}r_{t}
.

Then, we only need to upper-bound the regret after
T_{0}
. By Lemma
A.9.5
, we know that with probability at least
1-4\delta
, the algorithm can cluster all the users correctly,
\overline{C}_{t}=C_{j(i_{t})}
, and the statements of all the above lemmas hold. We have that for any
t\geq T_{0}
:

To simplify exposion here, we denote
\beta_{T}^{\prime}\triangleq\beta_{T}+B\sqrt{\lambda/\kappa_{\mu}}+1
.

\begin{split}r_{t}&=f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x}_{t,1})+f_{i_{t}}(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-f_{i_{t}}(x_{t,2})\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(a)}}{{\leq}}\langle g(\bm{x}^{*}_{t};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}_{t,1};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle+\beta_{T}^{\prime}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+\\
&\hskip 23.49976pt\langle g(\bm{x}^{*}_{t};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}_{t,2};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle+\beta_{T}^{\prime}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\\
&=\langle g(\bm{x}^{*}_{t};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}_{t,1};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle+\beta_{T}^{\prime}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+\\
&\hskip 23.49976pt\langle g(\bm{x}^{*}_{t};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}_{t,1};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle+\langle g(\bm{x}_{t,1};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}_{t,2};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle+\\
&\hskip 23.49976pt\beta_{T}^{\prime}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})+\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(b)}}{{\leq}}2\langle g(\bm{x}^{*}_{t};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}_{t,1};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle+2\beta_{T}^{\prime}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+\\
&\hskip 23.49976pt\langle g(\bm{x}_{t,1};\bm{\theta}_{0})-g(\bm{x}_{t,2};\bm{\theta}_{0}),\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t}-\bm{\theta}_{0}\rangle+\beta_{T}^{\prime}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(c)}}{{\leq}}2h(\bm{x}^{*}_{t};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})-2h(\bm{x}_{t,1};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})+4\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},t}+2\beta_{T}^{\prime}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}^{*}_{t})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+\\
&\hskip 23.49976pth(\bm{x}_{t,1};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})-h(\bm{x}_{t,2};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})+2\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},t}+\beta_{T}^{\prime}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(d)}}{{\leq}}2h(\bm{x}_{t,2};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})-2h(\bm{x}_{t,1};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})+2\beta_{T}^{\prime}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+\\
&\hskip 23.49976pth(\bm{x}_{t,1};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})-h(\bm{x}_{t,2};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})+6\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},t}+\beta_{T}^{\prime}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}\\
&=h(\bm{x}_{t,2};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})-h(\bm{x}_{t,1};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})+3\beta_{T}^{\prime}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+6\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},t}\\
&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(e)}}{{\leq}}3\beta_{T}^{\prime}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+6\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},t}\\
\end{split}

(A.360)

Step
(a)
follows from Equation
A.357
, step
(b)
results from the triangle inequality, step
(c)
has made use of Lemma
A.9.7
.
Step
(d)
follows from the way in which we choose the second arm
\bm{x}_{t,2}
:
\bm{x}_{t,2}=\arg\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{t}}h(\bm{x};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})+\left(\beta_{T}+B\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}+1\right)\left\lVert\left(\phi(\bm{x})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})\right)\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}
.
Step
(e)
results from the way in which we select the first arm:
\bm{x}_{t,1}=\arg\max_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{t}}h(\bm{x};\overline{\bm{\theta}}_{t})
.

Then we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=T_{0}}^{T}r_{t}

\displaystyle\leq 3\beta_{T}^{\prime}\sum_{t=T_{0}}^{T}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+6T\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},T}

\displaystyle=3\beta_{T}^{\prime}\sum_{t=T_{0}}^{T}\sum_{j\in[m]}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in C_{j}\}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}+6T\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},T}

\displaystyle\leq 3\beta_{T}^{\prime}\sqrt{\sum_{t=T_{0}}^{T}\sum_{j\in[m]}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in C_{j}\}\sum_{t=T_{0}}^{T}\sum_{j\in[m]}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in C_{j}\}\left\lVert\phi(\bm{x}_{t,1})-\phi(\bm{x}_{t,2})\right\rVert_{\bm{V}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}}

\displaystyle+6T\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},T}

\displaystyle\leq 3\beta_{T}^{\prime}\sqrt{T\cdot m\cdot 16\widetilde{d}}+6T\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},T}

(A.361)

\displaystyle\leq 12\beta_{T}^{\prime}\sqrt{T\cdot m\cdot\widetilde{d}}+6T\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},T}\,,

(A.362)

where in the second inequality we use the Cauchy-Swarchz inequality, and in the last step we use
\sum_{t=T_{0}}^{T}\sum_{j\in[m]}\mathbb{I}\{i_{t}\in C_{j}\}\leq T
and Lemma
A.9.9
.
It can be easily verified that as long as the conditions on
m
specified in
eq.

A.344
are satisfied (i.e., as long as the NN is wide enough), we have that

6T\varepsilon^{\prime}_{m_{\text{NN}},T}\leq 1
.

Recall that
\beta_{T}^{\prime}\triangleq\beta_{T}+B\sqrt{\lambda/\kappa_{\mu}}+1
and
\beta_{T}\triangleq\frac{1}{\kappa_{\mu}}\sqrt{\widetilde{d}+2\log(u/\delta)}
.
Therefore, finally, we have with probability at least
1-4\delta

\displaystyle R_{T}

\displaystyle\leq T_{0}+12(\beta_{T}+B\sqrt{\lambda/\kappa_{\mu}}+1)\sqrt{T\cdot m\cdot\widetilde{d}}+1

\displaystyle\leq O\left(u(\frac{\widetilde{d}}{\kappa_{\mu}^{2}\tilde{\lambda}_{x}\gamma^{2}}+\frac{1}{\tilde{\lambda}_{x}^{2}})\log T+\left(\frac{\sqrt{\widetilde{d}}}{\kappa_{\mu}}+B\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}\right)\sqrt{\widetilde{d}mT}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left(\left(\frac{\sqrt{\widetilde{d}}}{\kappa_{\mu}}+B\sqrt{\frac{\lambda}{\kappa_{\mu}}}\right)\sqrt{\widetilde{d}mT}\right)\,.
